# Decision log

Every decision that shapes the concept, newest last. **Nothing is a decision until it is in
this table.**

## Format

- **Id** — `D-<nnn>`, never reused. Legacy `Q<n>` ids are *not* continued here; if a decision
  reuses an old one, it is named under *Legacy source*.
- **Status** — `provisional` (recorded so work continues, but the owner is not convinced; carries a
  named doubt and a trigger for revisiting), `agreed` (owner confirmed) or `locked` (do not
  re-litigate without a new,
  superseding entry that names this one).
- A superseded entry is never deleted. It gets `superseded by D-<nnn>` in its status.

| Id | Date | Decision | Status | Affects | Legacy source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|

Reason: the old rules encoded half-finished model decisions as permanent coding rules and dragged the model back to them — the mechanism behind the repeated loops. | `agreed` | process | — |
| D-009 | 2026-08-22 | **Layered code style.** WordPress conventions at the boundary (hooks, REST, admin, activation, blocks — capabilities, nonces, sanitize/escape, text domain, `$wpdb->prefix`); modern PHP 8 in the core (namespaces, `declare(strict_types=1)`, PSR-4, typed properties). The core does not call WordPress functions. | `agreed` | [`CLAUDE.md`](../../CLAUDE.md) CD-1 | — |
| D-010 | 2026-08-22 | **One file per topic.** The model stays in a single document, [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md), including configuration and settings — matching how the owner had already laid out the seed sketches. Readability comes from the internal structure (a chain of small diagram units, per D-005 above), not from splitting files. The separate settings document is folded back in. | `agreed` | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md) | — |
| D-011 | 2026-08-22 | **A setting is an attribute.** Not a second concept: the settings of a node are the group of *additional* attributes it carries beyond the set every node has. Storage follows the split, not the concept — **fixed attributes** (every node has them) live on the node itself; **extended attributes** (arriving through specialisation, e.g. min/max/step on an integer node) live generically in the settings table. Display re-joins the two, fixed first, extended beneath. | `agreed` | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md) C3–C6, [50 Persistence](50-wordpress-persistence.md) | closes [OQ-013](91-open-questions.md); opens [OQ-016](91-open-questions.md), [OQ-017](91-open-questions.md), [OQ-018](91-open-questions.md) |
| D-012 | 2026-08-22 | **Inheritance is an edge — one construct, its own kind.** There is one `Relation`; inheritance is a distinct kind of it, not a separate class. Its special rules live as invariants (at most one parent, no cycles, not freely deletable), not as a second construct. | `agreed` | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md) | closes [OQ-023](91-open-questions.md); answers part 1 of [OQ-002](91-open-questions.md) |
| D-013 | 2026-08-22 | **The two resolutions must never be mixed.** *Inheritance resolution* — a node inherits attributes from its ancestors — and *settings resolution* — a node presents and overrides the settings of the nodes it reaches through composition and aggregation — are separate concepts with separate walks. This is also where the line runs between the free connections and inheritance. | `agreed` | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md) | — |
| D-014 | 2026-08-22 | **Load in one step.** The subgraph and every settings row it touches are loaded in a small fixed number of batched queries before resolution or rendering begins; neither resolver nor renderer touches the database. The ancestor walk is served by an indexed structure (materialised path or closure table), decided as a schema shape rather than added later as an optimisation. | `agreed` | [50 Persistence](50-wordpress-persistence.md), [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md) | answers [OQ-020](91-open-questions.md); part of [OQ-024](91-open-questions.md) |
| D-015 | 2026-08-22 | **Base settings live on the referenced node; the using attribute overrides sparsely.** Only what actually differs is stored on the edge. A change to a base setting therefore **propagates live** to every use site that has not overridden it — five nodes using `int` all follow a changed `step`, except the one that set its own. Attributes track their type; they do not freeze it. | `agreed` | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md) | closes [OQ-025](91-open-questions.md) in principle and [OQ-027](91-open-questions.md) for settings |
| D-016 | 2026-08-22 | **Caching is an implementation concern, bounded by two rules:** a cache is never a second source of truth, and it is updated when the data it derives from is written. Where to cache is decided during implementation, not in the concept. | `agreed` | process, [50 Persistence](50-wordpress-persistence.md) | narrows [OQ-024](91-open-questions.md) |
| D-017 | 2026-08-22 | **A part used in exactly one place stays an ordinary node**, reached by a composition edge, with an *add composed child here* action so building one is not tedious, and structure is never inlined as a second storage form. ~~It lives beneath its whole.~~ → **that part was wrong and is corrected by [D-135](#)**: the tree is inheritance only ([V3](00-vision-and-scope.md)), so *beneath its whole* would make a position inherit the parts list. | `agreed`, placement corrected by [D-135](#) | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md) | closes [OQ-026](91-open-questions.md) |
| D-018 | 2026-08-22 | **Renderer split: a completely different presentation is a separate renderer; a parameterisable detail is a renderer setting.** Field, spinner and slider are three renderers. A compact row renderer with *with label* / *without label* is one renderer with a setting. Renderers therefore carry settings of their own. | `agreed` | [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md) | refines [R15](30-renderer.md) |
| D-019 | 2026-08-22 | **Labels get their own table, separate from settings.** Base tables become **nodes, settings, labels, relations**. A setting is keyed by `key` and holds a typed value (min, max, step, hide, read_only, order, renderer choice, **icon** — which is language-neutral). A label is keyed by `role` + `locale` and holds text. Both remain *attributes* conceptually ([D-011](#)) and both resolve through the **same** walk — inherited, overridable per use site, sparse ([D-015](#)). One resolver, two tables. | `agreed` (owner objected to the single-table draft; reasoning in [40 I18n](40-i18n.md)) | [40 I18n](40-i18n.md), [50 Persistence](50-wordpress-persistence.md), `CLAUDE.md` AR-1 | amends [P4](50-wordpress-persistence.md) |
| D-020 | 2026-08-22 | ⚠️ **Überholt durch [D-209](#) und [D-386](#)** — was hier steht, gilt **nicht mehr unverändert**; die geltende Fassung steht dort. *Welcher Teil fiel, sagt die Zeile weiter unten.* **Every node carries a locale-neutral base name**, as a fixed attribute in the sense of [C4](10-domain-core.md) — always present, entered at creation, never translated. It is a label of last resort, **not** an identifier: nothing may look a node up by it. Label resolution falls back `label.<role>` → `label.long` → `node.name`, so a role only needs storing where it should genuinely differ, and a missing translation never yields an empty or key-shaped label. No placeholder value is ever stored; the fallback is computed. ⚠️ **`long` ist ersetzt** durch [D-209](#), die Kette dann durch [D-386](#); der neutrale Basisname gilt. | `agreed` (delegated by the owner: *think it through*) | [40 I18n](40-i18n.md), [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md) | answers [I10](40-i18n.md); feeds [OQ-017](91-open-questions.md) |
| D-021 | 2026-08-22 | **Renderers are PHP.** They produce markup and serve all three levels: admin screen, dynamic Gutenberg block output, and frontend — all three of which WordPress renders in PHP by convention. JavaScript is used only where interaction requires it and **never re-implements a renderer**. Because the Gutenberg editor is React by construction, its editing controls are **metadata-driven**: the editor receives a node's attributes with their types and settings and renders them with one generic control set, so a new node type costs no JavaScript. The renderer contract must therefore expose the attribute metadata it used, not only finished markup. Frontend interactivity uses the WordPress Interactivity API on server-rendered markup. | `agreed` | [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md), [`CLAUDE.md`](../../CLAUDE.md) | closes [OQ-014](91-open-questions.md); protects [R4](30-renderer.md) |
| D-022 | 2026-08-22 | **A node name is required and explicitly not unique.** Duplicates are a normal modelling outcome: two attributes may share a name, and two different nodes may each have a child of the same name. **Nothing resolves, references or branches on a name** — references use the id, which is stable while the name may change. Searching and sorting by name is fine. | `agreed` | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md), [40 I18n](40-i18n.md) | closes [OQ-032](91-open-questions.md); confirms [D-020](#) |
| D-023 | 2026-08-22 | **`Label` keeps its name; roles lose the prefix.** A role is `long` / `form` / `table` / `symbol`, never `label.form` — the prefix made *label* read as both the umbrella and one of its own parts, which confused the owner reading the diagram. Also: `owner` is drawn **explicitly** on `Setting`, `Label` and `ChangeLogItem`. The reference sits on the row and names the one identity it belongs to, node or edge; it is a single column only because ids come from one space ([C11](10-domain-core.md)). | `agreed` | [01 Glossary](01-glossary.md), [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md), [40 I18n](40-i18n.md) | — |
| D-024 | 2026-08-22 | **The concept is also published as a rendered page** in the style of the first one: small diagrams, explanation beneath, code snippets only where detail demands it. It is a *view* of `NewConcept/` and never a second source of truth. **Language:** German while the concept is being worked out, because that is the language the owner thinks in; translated to English together with the documents ([D-002](#)) once the concept is complete. | `agreed` | process, [98 Documentation style](98-documentation-style.md) | — |
| D-025 | 2026-08-22 | **The type of an attribute is the node the relation points to.** [C2](10-domain-core.md) named *type* and *connection kind* as two separate things; they are two fields of one edge — `to` is the type, `kind` is the connection. | `agreed` | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md) | closes [OQ-011](91-open-questions.md) |
| D-026 | 2026-08-22 | **Modelling view and data view are separate layers.** The model — nodes, relations, settings, labels — describes; an instance holds actual values. **At model level there are no values, only defaults.** A default may be a scalar *or* a whole record, i.e. the data of an instance. | `agreed` | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md), [50 Persistence](50-wordpress-persistence.md) | closes the model half of [OQ-018](91-open-questions.md); opens [OQ-035](91-open-questions.md), [OQ-036](91-open-questions.md); likely dissolves [OQ-033](91-open-questions.md) |
| D-027 | 2026-08-22 | **Three layers, not two:** the **model**, the **data** entered into it, and the **presentation** — the last being what the renderer concept covers. Extends [D-026](#), which named only the first two. | `agreed` | all | — |
| D-028 | 2026-08-22 | **Test data is ordinary data, flagged.** Rows can be marked as test data; the preview renders the node in the data view over those rows and falls back to the defaults where none exist. No separate test-data store and no third kind of thing. | `agreed` | [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md), [50 Persistence](50-wordpress-persistence.md) | closes [OQ-033](91-open-questions.md); simplifies [OQ-034](91-open-questions.md) |
| D-029 | 2026-08-22 | **The default editor is the data editor.** Entering a default for an attribute uses the same renderer in edit mode that entering real data uses; only the destination of the result differs. One editor, built once — [R1](30-renderer.md) and [R4](30-renderer.md) applied one level up. This is also what keeps defaults usable at higher multiplicity and for aggregations: the author is already familiar with the screen. | `agreed` | [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md), [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md) | — |
| D-030 | 2026-08-22 | **A default may be a reference, and there may be several.** Choosing options one and two out of four means two defaults, each an identity reference; multiplicity means several pre-filled rows. Stored as **settings whose value is an identity reference** — `Relation.from` and `Relation.to` keep pointing at nodes and nothing widens. | `agreed` | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md) | narrows [OQ-035](91-open-questions.md) to its smallest option |
| D-031 | 2026-08-22 | **An attribute *is* a relation.** No separate construct: the edge carries the connection kind (`kind`), the type (`to`, per [D-025](#)), the name (`Relation.name` plus labels), the multiplicity and the defaults (settings on the edge, per [C8](10-domain-core.md)). **The wrapper is real but lives in the user interface** — one dialog creates one relation row plus a handful of settings rows. ⚠️ *Recorded from a dictated "ja"; correct this entry if that reading was wrong.* | `agreed` | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md), [01 Glossary](01-glossary.md) | closes [OQ-010](91-open-questions.md) |
| D-032 | 2026-08-22 | **The two-fold principle.** Wherever a behaviour can sensibly vary, there are two levels: a **default configured in the admin menu**, and the **choice the user makes in the moment**. Both must exist — the configured default alone is rigid, the choice alone is tedious. Applies to inline-versus-dialog selection, the renderer choice, and the handling of orphaned overrides. | `agreed` | all | — |
| D-033 | 2026-08-22 | **Orphaned overrides are never cascade-deleted.** When the thing an override pointed at is removed, the user decides: delete them, or **promote the override into an attribute of its own** at the level where it was overridden. Promotion is the more interesting option — if a value was worth overriding, losing its target does not remove the need for it. | `agreed` | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md) | closes the remainder of [OQ-025](91-open-questions.md); opens [OQ-037](91-open-questions.md) |
| D-034 | 2026-08-22 | **Node selection is one unified interaction**, offered either inline in the settings or through a dialog. The preferred way is configured in the admin menu; a particular place may require the dialog. An instance of [D-032](#). | `agreed` | [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md) | — |
| D-035 | 2026-08-22 | **A chooser takes two nodes:** a **branch node** whose subtree it shows, and a **default node** down to whose children it expands. The user picks from those children but may navigate into any other visible branch. The branch scopes the choice; the default is a landing point, not a boundary. | `agreed` | [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md) | legacy design named in [`ARCHITECTURE.md`](../legacy/ARCHITECTURE.md) is a cross-check only — the owner warns it contains wrong conclusions |
| D-036 | 2026-08-22 | **⚠️ Die Repräsentationshälfte ist ersetzt durch [D-484](#): der Eigentümer will **spezialisierte Knotenklassen**, «weil dann auch klar ist, wie viele spezialisierten Typen wir haben». *Diese Zeile stand auf `agreed (delegated)` und hat die Korrektur ausdrücklich angeboten — «korrigiere es, falls das nicht die Absicht war». **Sie war es nicht.** Die Strategien-Hälfte bleibt: eine Klasse kann Verhalten tragen und trotzdem eine registrierte Strategie benutzen. Und der Teil über die **Relationsarten als Enum** ist unberührt.* Ersetzt am 2026-08-28.** ~~Relation kinds are an enum~~, and **nodes are one class** with type-specific behaviour in registered strategies and optional typed accessors — the recommendations argued in [OQ-003](91-open-questions.md) and [OQ-004](91-open-questions.md). ⚠️ *Delegated: the owner said the representation does not matter to them. Recorded so work continues; correct it if that was not the intent.* | `agreed` (delegated) | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md) | closes [OQ-003](91-open-questions.md), [OQ-004](91-open-questions.md) |
| D-037 | 2026-08-22 | **A model change is breaking or not depending on the existing data, not on the kind of change.** Lowering an integer maximum from 100 to 20 breaks nothing if no stored value exceeds 20. Adding a field leaves old records empty, which is only a break if the field is mandatory — and then the author may state how old records are to be filled. So versions divide into **breaking** ones, which the user must resolve, and **continued** ones, which pass through. Which one a change is, is decided by **checking the data**, and the resolution happens through **rules and dialogs**. | `agreed` | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md), [OQ-031](91-open-questions.md) | reframes [OQ-001](91-open-questions.md) part 4 |

| D-038 | 2026-08-22 | **Relation kinds do not belong in the tree.** The old `Relationstypen` branch existed only to show which kinds there are. Confirms [D-036](#): kinds are an enum in code, not nodes. | `agreed` | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md) | harvest 01 B1/B2 → drop |
| D-039 | 2026-08-22 | **A unit value is one notion**: value + optional prefix + unit. Which part varies follows from its sense — a currency changes its **unit** (euro to dollar), a length changes its **prefix** (metre to kilometre). Currencies and base units are two branches of one shape, not two mechanisms. | `agreed` | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md) | closes [OQ-040](91-open-questions.md); harvest 01 A2/B6 |
| D-040 | 2026-08-22 | **Permitted prefixes are an allow-list setting on the attribute**, not a branch split and not a hidden control. Metre permits milli/centi/kilo and not giga; Kelvin permits the empty set, so no control appears. Generic and author-definable, as the owner required after the previous attempt hard-coded a type and kept breaking. | `agreed` | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md) | answers the owner question in C49; harvest 01 A3 reworked |
| D-041 | 2026-08-22 | **The type of an attribute is a branch, and the branch is polymorphic.** Any descendant may stand where the branch root is named. Data entry is therefore **multi-step**: choose a node from the branch, then fill its attributes if it has any. An attribute may **narrow** the branch (see [D-040](#)). This makes the chooser and the type system one mechanism, and makes a choice list an attribute whose branch is shallow. | `agreed` | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md), [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md) | closes [OQ-042](91-open-questions.md) |
| D-042 | 2026-08-22 | **Type node and model node are the same construct in different roles.** A composed type is a small model; a model is a large type. Nothing in the engine tells them apart. This is a different axis from [D-026](#), which separates the model layer from the data layer — both hold at once. **The renderer concept exists because of this**: if a model is a deeply composed type, entering and showing one is the hard problem, and [R1](30-renderer.md) is the answer to it. | `agreed` | all | strengthens [OQ-004](91-open-questions.md); opens [OQ-048](91-open-questions.md) |
| D-043 | 2026-08-22 | **A calculation is a property of an attribute, not a relation kind.** An attribute whose value comes from an expression instead of from input. Dependencies may be materialised as edges **for invalidation only**, derived from the expression and never authored. Two owners, one expression language: a **model calculation** belongs to an attribute and yields a value; a **display calculation** belongs to a renderer and yields output only. **Unit conversion and text transformation are converters, not calculations** — a calculation produces a value that did not exist, a converter changes the form of one that did. | `agreed` (design proposal, owner asked for it) | [60 Calculation](60-calculation.md) | answers [OQ-044](91-open-questions.md); refuses [K5](60-calculation.md) |
| D-044 | 2026-08-22 | **`display_node_name` is a renderer, not a data type.** Showing the name of a referenced node — with a choice of which label role to use — is exactly a renderer over a node reference, configured by a setting. It needs no pseudo-type. | `agreed` | [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md), [40 I18n](40-i18n.md) | harvest 01 B5 → drop, reworked as a renderer |

| D-045 | 2026-08-22 | **A calculation names its inputs by relative edge-id path** — the same addressing overrides already use ([OQ-025](91-open-questions.md)). An expression is an **operation** plus **operands**, each operand a path. `Position.preis = multiply([#88],[#93])`; `Bauteilliste.gesamtpreis = sum([#42,#95])`. Two consequences: **scalar or aggregate is not declared but implied** by the multiplicity along the path, so the model can catch a `multiply` pointed at a collection; and the **dependency graph is parsed out of the expressions**, materialised only as an index for invalidation, never authored. This is why the old `calc` edge existed — it was the only pointing mechanism available at the time. | `agreed` (design proposal) | [60 Calculation](60-calculation.md) | answers [OQ-045](91-open-questions.md); harvest 01 B7 |
| D-046 | 2026-08-22 | **Permitted sub-nodes are a setting holding a mode plus a list** — *all except {…}* or *only {…}*. The owner's mechanism (tick the branch's inheriting sub-nodes on or off at the attribute) is the same thing and stays the interface. The mode is needed because pure opt-out makes *no prefix at all* awkward (Kelvin would silently gain every newly added prefix) while pure opt-in stops new prefixes reaching metre automatically. Default *all except {}*. Inheritable and overridable per use site; never a coded type. | `agreed` | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md) | refines [D-040](#) |
| D-047 | 2026-08-22 | **`Gramm` is the base unit; the prefix carries the kilo.** The SI base for mass is the kilogram, but this model calls them **Basiseinheiten**, not SI units, and a prefixable base has to be the unprefixed one. This is what the previous project had built before the tree was repeatedly damaged. | `agreed` | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md) | harvest 01 B3 |
| D-048 | 2026-08-22 | **The useful axis is *when is the content determined*, not *type versus model*.** Three positions: **never** (Integer, Text), **at modelling time** (Präfixe, Bauformen, Basiseinheiten — data authored in the modeller), **at input time** (a parts list). The middle case is what the standard tree calls `Konstanten`; it is neither a type in the sense `Integer` is, nor user input. Sharpens [D-042](#). | `agreed` | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md), [01 Glossary](01-glossary.md) | half-answers [OQ-048](91-open-questions.md) |
| D-049 | 2026-08-22 | **A unit reference is data; the text for it is a label.** Correcting the wording of [D-044](#) after the owner objected: that a value is *in Stück* is stored, as a reference from the `einheit` attribute to a node — the owner is right that it belongs to the record. What is **not** stored is the string: whether that node reads `St`, `Stück` or `pcs` is a label in a role and locale, resolved at render time. `display_node_name` bundled *make the reference visible* with *choose which text*; only the second half is presentation, and it is a **setting on the renderer** naming the label role. | `agreed` | [40 I18n](40-i18n.md), [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md) | clarifies [D-044](#); opens [OQ-049](91-open-questions.md) |

| D-050 | 2026-08-22 | **Do not ask what cannot matter.** Before a question about consequences is put to the user, the system checks whether any consequences exist: if nothing uses a type, the activation-versus-deactivation question is not asked; if no data exist, a model change cannot be breaking. Both are queries the system answers for itself. Extends [D-032](#) with a step in front, and is what keeps a dialog-driven design from becoming exhausting. **Test data are the carved-out exception: warn, name which are affected, do not block.** | `agreed` | all | — |
| D-051 | 2026-08-22 | **A unit value is stored in its base unit, with the chosen prefix beside it as the display form.** Input and output convert at the renderer boundary — the **converter** of [V8](00-vision-and-scope.md), as [D-043](#) already classified it. Reason: aggregation ([D-045](#)) sums across collections, and `5 kg + 300 g` needs a common scale; stored-as-entered makes every aggregate conversion-dependent and a missed conversion silently wrong. Changing the prefix then alters only the **display**, so the owner concern about having to rewrite entered values disappears. Values are decimal or scaled integer, never float. **Exception: a prefix normalises, a unit does not** — a currency amount stays in its currency, because an exchange rate is a time series ([C48](10-domain-core.md)). | `agreed` (design proposal; owner: *the data must go in correctly and come back out correctly*) | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md), [50 Persistence](50-wordpress-persistence.md) | — |
| D-052 | 2026-08-22 | **Test data have three sources, in fallback order:** a record flagged as test data ([D-028](#)), otherwise the attribute defaults ([D-030](#)) assembled into a sample, otherwise generated from the settings (type, min, max, step). A new type therefore previews immediately with nothing entered, and flagging a record is done in the ordinary data editor with a checkbox ([D-029](#)). | `agreed` | [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md) | closes the remainder of [OQ-033](91-open-questions.md) |

| D-053 | 2026-08-22 | **Rename is not replace.** A rename or relabel touches a **label**: the reference is unchanged, so the data are unchanged and only the wording of the output differs — **keep it current, never freeze**. Replacing or removing a node touches a **reference**: that changes the model version and produces a conflict in existing data, which the user resolves. **Documents do not argue against this**: an exported PDF is detached the moment it is produced, and regenerating it later giving a different document is expected rather than wrong. | `agreed` | [70 Migration](70-migration.md), [40 I18n](40-i18n.md) | closes [OQ-049](91-open-questions.md) |
| D-054 | 2026-08-22 | **The conflict resolver is the mechanism for model change.** It lists which models have conflicts with their data and lets the user resolve them; afterwards the data have been carried into the new structure. Resolution **loops** — after each step the data are re-checked, so a model that changed several times is worked down stage by stage. **There is no silent migration behind it.** This makes [D-037](#) operable, and [D-050](#) applies: with no data present there is nothing to check and nothing to ask. | `agreed` | [70 Migration](70-migration.md) | gives [OQ-031](91-open-questions.md) its mechanism |
| D-055 | 2026-08-22 | **Data reference node ids, never names** — restated as a data-layer rule, not only a modelling one. Renaming a node changes every output and no stored value. | `agreed` | [70 Migration](70-migration.md), [50 Persistence](50-wordpress-persistence.md) | confirms [D-022](#) at the data layer |

| D-056 | 2026-08-22 | **A control offers only real choices.** Never present a decision the circumstances have already made. Zero possible answers → the control is disabled; exactly one → preselected and greyed; more than one → an ordinary control. Whether *nothing* counts as an answer follows from the multiplicity: `0..1` and `0..*` may be empty, `1` and `1..*` may not. **One available entry with an optional multiplicity keeps a real choice** — take it or leave it — and must not be greyed. This is [D-050](#) at widget scale; together they make one rule running from a whole dialog down to a single field. | `agreed` | [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md) | — |
| D-057 | 2026-08-22 | **Numbers are whole or decimal, never floating point.** Calculate and store at **full precision**; display in a readable form. The converter sits between the two, exactly as in [D-051](#) — that decision along the unit axis, this one along the precision axis. Aggregation runs on stored values, never on displayed ones, because summing individually rounded amounts accumulates the error. **A rounding rule may not turn a nonzero amount into a displayed zero** — below a visible cent, something owed must still read as something owed. | `agreed` | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md), [50 Persistence](50-wordpress-persistence.md) | — |

| D-058 | 2026-08-22 | **Two exports, kept apart.** A **backup export** carries the tree *and* the data together, must round-trip, and writes **both the id and the plain text** of every reference — so the file is readable without the tool, survives the loss of a referenced node, and is self-describing. A **view export** — CSV, PDF, an interactive parts list — is a **renderer** ([R1](30-renderer.md)) and need not round-trip. They pull in opposite directions (completeness versus readability), so one artefact must not try to be both. | `agreed` | [70 Migration](70-migration.md), [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md) | closes [OQ-050](91-open-questions.md) |
| D-059 | 2026-08-22 | **Import resolution is the conflict resolver.** An import that meets a reference to a node which no longer exists produces a conflict, and the user resolves it by **mapping to an existing node** or **creating one and binding the id**. No second mechanism, and **an import never silently invents nodes**. | `agreed` | [70 Migration](70-migration.md) | — |
| D-060 | 2026-08-22 | ⚠️ **Überholt durch [D-210](#)** — was hier steht, gilt **nicht mehr unverändert**; die geltende Fassung steht dort. *Welcher Teil fiel, sagt die Zeile weiter unten.* **The model version is carried by the record, not by `Identity`.** Every record states which model shape it was written against; resolving advances it. Records of different versions coexist until everything is resolved, after which all sit at the current version. **This settles the hard half of [OQ-001](91-open-questions.md):** the row change counter and the model version are different numbers belonging to different things — one to a node, one to a record. ⚠️ **Der Schlusssatz ist ersetzt** durch [D-210](#); der Rest gilt. | `agreed` | [70 Migration](70-migration.md), [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md) | closes [OQ-051](91-open-questions.md); unblocks [OQ-001](91-open-questions.md) |
| D-061 | 2026-08-22 | **Migration needs the changes, not the snapshots — and the changelog is the migration script.** Carrying a record from v1 to v3 requires knowing *what changed at each step*, not what the whole model looked like. `ChangeLogItem`, present since the seed sketch without a job, is exactly that list. This is the first real reason for it to exist, and it argues for logging every change rather than treating the log as optional. | `agreed` | [70 Migration](70-migration.md), [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md) | bears on [OQ-008](91-open-questions.md) |
| D-062 | 2026-08-22 | **The resolver offers: map, map with transformation, bulk fill, fill by hand, delete.** A transformation is a **converter** ([D-043](#)) applied to a column — gram to kilogram is a unit conversion, `Stück` to `STK` a text rewrite — so the resolver needs no transformation language of its own, and every converter written for display is available for migration. | `agreed` | [70 Migration](70-migration.md) | closes [OQ-052](91-open-questions.md) |
| D-063 | 2026-08-22 | **A change that creates a new version warns at the moment it is made**, not later on a list. The message has a fixed shape — *the existing records still hold this field; the new shape does not* — and two answers: **delete** or **transfer**. [D-050](#) applies: only when data exist to be affected. Deciding then is far easier than deciding weeks later, because the reason for the change is still in the author's head. | `agreed` | [70 Migration](70-migration.md) | — |

| D-064 | 2026-08-22 | **An amount is stored in its own currency; a frozen rate is stored beside it; the converted figure is derived.** Euro normalisation was not intended — dollars are stored as dollars. Where a price must stay put (an order agreed at ten dollars, eight euros fifty that day), the **rate of that day is stored**, not the converted result: storing only the result loses what was agreed, and storing both records one fact twice. **Exception, to be invoked explicitly:** where a converted figure is legally fixed — a posted invoice, a booking — the booked amount may have to be stored as well, so it cannot shift if a rounding rule is later corrected. **Whether an amount freezes its rate is a setting on the attribute** ([D-032](#)); the record carries the frozen rate. | `agreed` | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md), [50 Persistence](50-wordpress-persistence.md) | closes [OQ-054](91-open-questions.md) |
| D-065 | 2026-08-22 | **Track what describes, freeze what was agreed.** A label is a *name* for a thing, so it tracks ([D-053](#)); a rate is a *term of an agreement*, so it freezes ([D-064](#)). The rule is neither *always track* nor *always freeze*, and this is the test that separates the two cases. | `agreed` | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md), [40 I18n](40-i18n.md) | — |

| D-066 | 2026-08-22 | **No table per model. The model is the schema.** Data are stored generically and the model says how to read them — the same shape the settings table already has, one layer down, and what makes a run-time modeller possible at all. **Consequence to design in, not discover:** no query can filter on a domain field without going through the model, so reporting-style queries are model lookups plus generic filters, served by [D-014](#)'s batched loading and indexed walks. | `agreed` | [50 Persistence](50-wordpress-persistence.md) | shapes [OQ-015](91-open-questions.md) |
| D-067 | 2026-08-22 | **The model declares one reference currency; a frozen rate always points at it.** This removes the ambiguity the owner named: a rate is between *two* currencies and only one had been fixed. What is held is the currency **agreed**; the rate means *this currency → the reference currency* at the moment of freezing; and when the amount is already in the reference currency the rate is 1 and nothing is stored. The reference currency is a setting, per model or per installation ([OQ-039](91-open-questions.md)). | `agreed` | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md) | resolves C75 |
| D-068 | 2026-08-22 | **The frozen rate belongs to the money type, not to a hand-made hidden field.** It appears when freezing is switched on for that attribute. A field the author must remember to add would be forgotten, and forgetting it loses the rate **silently** — the amount still looks right and only later turns out to be unreproducible. Structure, not convention. | `agreed` | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md) | refines [C74](10-domain-core.md) |
| D-069 | 2026-08-22 | **Rates are fetched at the boundary into a rate table; the core only reads the table.** Satisfies `CD-1` — the core never calls out — and it means an unreachable service leaves yesterday's rate in place instead of breaking anything. **Daily granularity, no intraday.** Basis: the European Central Bank publishes euro reference rates once per working day, and German VAT practice uses *monthly* average rates, so daily is already finer than what is expected. The freezing is what protects the number anyway ([D-064](#)), not the granularity of the source. The table needs a rule for days without a published rate — *use the last published one* — and rate values carry four to six decimal places under [D-057](#). | `agreed` | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md), [50 Persistence](50-wordpress-persistence.md) | closes [OQ-055](91-open-questions.md) |

| D-070 | 2026-08-22 | **Search is a requirement, not an aspiration.** *All BOMs over a thousand euro*, *all BOMs containing a particular part*, *all parts inside a particular BOM* — near-relational capability on a finer-grained store. Two capabilities follow: an **indexed value lookup** and an **indexed edge traversal in both directions**. Because an attribute is a relation with a stable id ([D-031](#)), the edge id plays the part a column name plays, and a filter becomes an ordinary indexed range scan. | `agreed` | [50 Persistence](50-wordpress-persistence.md) | shapes [OQ-015](91-open-questions.md) |
| D-071 | 2026-08-22 | **Values are stored in typed columns, never in one stringly `value`.** A numeric column for numbers, text for text, a reference column for node references, a date column for dates. With everything as text, `'900' > '1000'` is true and ranges cannot be indexed usefully — [D-070](#) would quietly become impossible. This has to be settled before the data tables exist, which is why it is a concept decision and not an implementation one. | `agreed` | [50 Persistence](50-wordpress-persistence.md) | — |
| D-072 | 2026-08-22 | **Computed values are materialised**, written when their inputs change and read like any other value. Forced by [D-070](#): filtering on a computed total that is derived on read would mean computing it for every candidate record, which is the scan the requirement rules out. This makes the invalidation index of [D-045](#) load-bearing rather than an optimisation — a stale total is now a **wrong search result**, not merely a stale display. | `agreed` | [60 Calculation](60-calculation.md), [50 Persistence](50-wordpress-persistence.md) | answers [OQ-046](91-open-questions.md) |
| D-073 | 2026-08-22 | **A currency value is an ordinary composed node with registered behaviour** — value, currency, and a frozen rate where freezing is on — **not a subclass**, per [D-036](#). **Which currencies exist is model data** determined at modelling time ([D-048](#)), not an enum. **Each currency declares its minor unit** (euro 2, yen 0, Kuwaiti dinar 3), and that minor unit governs **display and rounding, not storage**: amounts are stored with more places than the minor unit so sub-cent values survive ([C69](10-domain-core.md)), and rounded to it only on the way out. | `agreed` | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md) | — |

| D-074 | 2026-08-22 | **Separate typed value columns, including whole numbers apart from decimals** — `value_int`, `value_decimal`, `value_text`, `value_ref`, `value_date`. The objection that this makes every numeric query hit two indexes **does not hold**: `edge_id` already pins the type, because an attribute is one edge with one target type, so a query knows which column it means before it starts. Benefits kept: exact whole-number semantics, a smaller integer index, no silent coercion. **Type safety is enforced by the type node's validator first and by the column last** — a user should meet the validator, never a database error. | `agreed` | [50 Persistence](50-wordpress-persistence.md) | refines [D-071](#) |
| D-075 | 2026-08-22 | **A numeral-system change is a converter** — binary, hexadecimal, octal, Roman. By the test of [D-043](#): `XII` and `12` are the same number written differently, so nothing is gained or lost. | `agreed` | [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md) | — |
| D-076 | 2026-08-22 | **Converters divide into invertible and lossy, and only invertible ones may serve input or search.** Gram ↔ kilogram, decimal ↔ Roman, decimal ↔ hex are invertible: typing `> XII` parses to `12` before the query runs. Rounding, truncation and case changes are lossy and are **display only**. **Consequence: a search never runs against the rounded form** — `> 8.50` is evaluated on the stored value, or a row showing `8.50` while holding `8.4999` answers wrongly. | `agreed` | [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md), [50 Persistence](50-wordpress-persistence.md) | — |
| D-077 | 2026-08-22 | **A node may carry several converters; which one applies is a setting.** The node holds a default and a use site may override it — [D-032](#) again, and the same shape as the renderer choice. A converter is therefore registered and chosen, not a fixed property. **Answers the second part of [OQ-007](91-open-questions.md): *one converter* was not a hard limit.** | `agreed` | [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md) | — |

| D-078 | 2026-08-22 | ⚠️ **Veraltet, ersetzt durch [D-084](#).** Der Wortlaut steht in [92 Veraltete Entscheidungen](92-veraltete-entscheidungen.md#d-078) — hier bleibt nur die Adresse, weil Code und Doku diese Id zitieren. | `veraltet` | — | — |
| D-079 | 2026-08-22 | **An installation-wide default is a setting on a reserved installation identity**, and that identity is the first link of the resolution chain: installation → model root → ancestors → node → use site. **This makes [D-032](#) fall out of [D-015](#) with no new machinery** — the configured default and the choice in the moment are the top and the bottom of one walk that already exists. The installation identity is reserved and invisible in the modeller. Genuinely WordPress-shaped settings (which admin page, which capability) stay WordPress options at the boundary. | `agreed` (confirmed by the owner 2026-08-22) | [50 Persistence](50-wordpress-persistence.md) | closes [OQ-039](91-open-questions.md) |
| D-080 | 2026-08-22 | ⚠️ **Überholt durch [D-436](#)** — was hier steht, gilt **nicht mehr unverändert**; die geltende Fassung steht dort. *Welcher Teil fiel, sagt die Zeile weiter unten.* **`Identity` carries `id` and `version` only.** Named `Identity`, not `WPClassHead`, because `CD-1` forbids the core knowing WordPress exists. **`type` is not on it** — a node's type *is* its inheritance branch ([D-041](#)), and a relation has its own `kind`. **`version` stays as a row change counter** for optimistic locking and cache invalidation, now clearly distinct from the model version that [D-060](#) put on the record. **`creation_date` comes off and is derived from the changelog** ⚠️ *my call* — if listing trees proves to need it, it becomes a materialised column like a computed value ([D-072](#)), a cache and not a second truth. ⚠️ **Das «only» ist ersetzt** durch [D-436](#) — `name` gehoert dazu, weil Knoten und Kante beide einen tragen und beide ihn uebersetzbar machen ([D-410](#)). *Diese Entscheidung wurde geschrieben, bevor ein Attribut Labels hatte.* Und [D-437](#) trennt, was [D-091](#) vermischt hatte: eine `Identity` zu sein und **zeichenbar** zu sein sind zwei Dinge. | `agreed` (confirmed by the owner 2026-08-23) | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md) | closes [OQ-001](91-open-questions.md) |
| D-081 | 2026-08-22 | **Every object has at least one changelog item — the seed's `1..*` was right.** If the changelog is the migration script ([D-061](#)) and `creation_date` is derived from it ([D-080](#)), creation must always be logged. **`undo()` is deliberately deferred**: the changelog enables it, nothing so far requires it. | `agreed` | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md) | closes [OQ-008](91-open-questions.md); opens [OQ-057](91-open-questions.md) |
| D-082 | 2026-08-22 | **Every node has exactly four fixed attributes: `id`, `version`, `name`, `path`.** `path` is the materialised ancestor path — derived and rebuildable. Everything else that looked like a candidate belongs elsewhere: **`type`** is the inheritance branch, **`order`** belongs to the *edge* because ordering is per parent, and hide / read-only / renderer / converter choices are system-scope settings. | `agreed` (proposal) | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md), [50 Persistence](50-wordpress-persistence.md) | closes [OQ-017](91-open-questions.md) |
| D-083 | 2026-08-22 | ⚠️ **Überholt durch [D-133](#)** — was hier steht, gilt **nicht mehr unverändert**; die geltende Fassung steht dort. *Welcher Teil fiel, sagt die Zeile weiter unten.* **Seven tables.** Model: `nodes` · `relations` · `settings` · `labels` · `changelog`. Data: `records` · `record_values`. ~~A composed value is a record of its own, referenced through `value_ref`.~~ → **that half superseded by [D-133](#)**: where a value is stored is decided by the relation kind and the multiplicity. | `agreed`, storage rule superseded by [D-133](#) | [50 Persistence](50-wordpress-persistence.md) | closes [OQ-015](91-open-questions.md) |


| D-084 | 2026-08-22 | **Settings are one construct with one mechanism and a reserved namespace. Supersedes [D-078](#).** The owner rejected the model-scope / system-scope split: the axis that matters is not *what kind of setting* but **where it is defined and where it may be overridden**, and that is identical for all of them. `min` is set on the integer node and narrowed at a use site; **the renderer, converter and validators are likewise set on the node as initial values** and overridden at a use site. One mechanism, no `scope` column. **What survives:** some keys are engine-owned, so those names are **reserved**, validated at write time, and an author cannot define a setting called `hide` and silently break rendering. **Why the column was wrong:** its justification was letting a consumer fetch only its own scope, and consumers do not partition that way — a renderer wants `hide` *and* the renderer choice, a validator wants `min` *and* the validator choice. A few keys only *apply* on an edge (multiplicity), which is applicability, not a second mechanism. ⚠️ *This row was lost to a failed edit on 2026-08-22 and restored after a gap check; the reasoning is intact in [50 Persistence](50-wordpress-persistence.md).* | `agreed` | [50 Persistence](50-wordpress-persistence.md), [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md) | closes [OQ-016](91-open-questions.md) |
| D-085 | 2026-08-22 | **The distinction is engine-owned versus type-owned keys, not *does the engine branch on it*.** The owner corrected the phrasing: a renderer must honour `min` and `max` too — a spinner cannot be drawn without them. What differs is **who owns the meaning**: the engine defines `hide`, `read_only`, `renderer`, `converter`, `validators` identically for every node; a **type** defines `min`, `max`, `step`, and a spinner reads them because it is registered *for that type*. Engine-owned names are reserved and validated at write time. | `agreed` | [50 Persistence](50-wordpress-persistence.md) | refines [D-084](#) |
| D-086 | 2026-08-22 | **Edge-only settings exist; node-only settings do not.** *Everything sayable about a node is also sayable about one use of it; the reverse is not true.* A node describes a thing, an edge describes a use of it — so any property of the thing can be restated for one use, which is what overriding is, while some things can only be said about a use. **Edge-defining:** `kind` and the attribute `name` (fields on `relations`) and **multiplicity**. This is why settings resolve **node → edge** and never the other way. **Multiplicity stays a setting, not a column**, because it inherits and can be narrowed, and a setting gets the resolution walk for free; denormalising later would be a cache. | `agreed` | [50 Persistence](50-wordpress-persistence.md), [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md) | — |

| D-087 | 2026-08-22 | **An override is the same thing wherever it sits; only its owner differs.** Owner **node**: *for this subtype* the inherited attribute is narrowed, hidden or reconfigured — a child may hide inherited attributes or override their properties. Owner **edge**: *for this one use*, the same. Both address the target as a path of edge ids ([D-045](#)) and both are read by the walk that already passes ancestors and then the use site ([D-079](#)). **[C9](10-domain-core.md) stays intact — the inheritance edge carries nothing;** the override hangs on the node and names the inherited edge by id. **And there is no type-definition view**, because a type is a node: configuring a type is configuring a node ([D-042](#)). | `agreed` | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md) | closes [OQ-058](91-open-questions.md) |

| D-088 | 2026-08-22 | **An override may narrow *and* widen. No monotonicity rule.** Overriding `1` with `0..1` makes an attribute optional here; overriding `0..1` with `1` makes it required here. The reason widening breaks nothing: **multiplicity — and every other constraint — is a statement about a *use*, not about a *thing*, and is evaluated where it resolves.** There is no global guarantee to break, because nothing elsewhere assumed the tighter value. **This removes a concept:** the proposed *constraint-like versus presentation-like* marking of keys is unnecessary and is dropped. **Modelling rule that follows:** anything that must hold always and everywhere belongs in the **type hierarchy**, not in an attribute constraint, because any use site may loosen the latter. | **superseded by [D-312](#)** | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md) | closes [OQ-059](91-open-questions.md) |

| D-089 | 2026-08-22 | **Concurrency, answered per layer.** **Data layer: optimistic, always** — compare `version` on save, refuse and show what changed; concurrent entry is the normal case and must not be blocked. **Model layer: optimistic as the mechanism, plus a heartbeat lease as a *courtesy warning*** (*X is currently editing this node*) that is **advisory, not enforcement** — the warning arrives before the work is done, and nothing jams if a laptop is closed. **Parallel work is not forbidden.** `version` is bumped by any write to an identity or to the rows it owns (settings, labels), so concurrent editing is detected even when two people touch different settings of one node — deliberately coarse, so no change is ever silently lost. **WordPress input:** `wp_set_post_lock()` / `wp_check_post_lock()` implement exactly the lease-with-expiry-and-take-over pattern for posts and are not usable here ([D-007](#)), but the **Heartbeat API is** — and it is the awkward part to build oneself. WordPress offers no optimistic locking at all; the `version` comparison is entirely ours. **With a single modeller none of this has teeth** ([D-050](#)), so the courtesy warning can wait for a second person. | `agreed` | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md), [50 Persistence](50-wordpress-persistence.md) | closes [OQ-060](91-open-questions.md) |

| D-090 | 2026-08-22 | **Every foreign key column ends in `_id`.** `owner_id`, `from_id`, `to_id`, `model_id`, `record_id`, `edge_id`, `by_user_id`. Raised by the owner, who read `owner` as a name rather than as a reference — which is exactly the confusion the suffix prevents. **Exception by pattern:** the value columns stay `value_int`, `value_decimal`, `value_text`, `value_ref`, `value_date`, because there the suffix names the **type**, and a reference is an id by nature. **`owner_id` rather than `node_id`** because the same column also holds relation ids — one number space ([C11](10-domain-core.md)) means one column and a foreign key the database can actually check. | `agreed` | [50 Persistence](50-wordpress-persistence.md) | — |

| D-091 | 2026-08-22 | **The render descent, and one contract for both.** The registry's `render` is an **entry point** only. From a node: resolve the renderer **name**, fetch the renderer from the registry, render the node's own properties, then hand each **edge** onward the same way; the target node starts the walk again. **Both nodes and edges are renderable** — and that is not a break, because both are an `Identity` carrying a resolved renderer setting. `render(Identity $subject, RenderContext $context): RenderResult`, plus `supports()` for the choice list. `RenderContext` carries the loaded subgraph, level, editable, resolved settings and locale; `RenderResult` carries markup **and** the attribute metadata used ([D-021](#)). **`renderTable` / `renderForm` disappear** — variants are separate renderers ([D-018](#)); `IPageRendere` is unnecessary, a page is a rendered node. **Resolution order:** the edge's own setting → the target node's → its ancestors → a fallback — [D-079](#)'s walk, confirmed for the renderer choice. | `agreed` | [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md) | closes [OQ-006](91-open-questions.md), [OQ-005](91-open-questions.md) |
| D-092 | 2026-08-22 | **R3 answered: a node renderer renders exactly one node; an edge renderer handles one value or many.** An earlier proposal had the context carry a *list of entries* so one renderer could serve either — heavier than needed. **Multiplicity belongs to the edge** ([D-086](#)), so one-or-many is handled where it already lives. | `agreed` | [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md) | supersedes the list-of-entries idea |

| D-093 | 2026-08-22 | **Every setting resolves on its own key.** The configuration reaching a renderer is assembled **key by key** along the walk, never taken wholesale from whichever level happened to say something: an edge that overrides `max` but not the renderer leaves the renderer coming from the node. The natural mistake is the other one — *the edge overrode something, so take everything from the edge* — which would silently discard the node's renderer choice and every setting the edge did not mention. Same rule as sparse overrides ([D-015](#)), seen from the reading side: sparse storage only works if reading merges per key. | `agreed` | [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md), [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md) | — |
| D-094 | 2026-08-22 | **Ground rule: when rendering, honour every attribute — no special arrangements, the same everywhere.** A renderer takes account of `hide`, `read_only`, the chosen converter, `min`, `max`, `step`, multiplicity and the label in the right role and locale — **all** of them, not the ones it happens to care about. A renderer that ignores `hide` produces a visible field the model calls invisible, and the bug surfaces somewhere else entirely. Pairs with [D-056](#): one rule says never present a decision already made, this one says never ignore what the model stated. | `agreed` | [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md) | — |
| D-095 | 2026-08-22 | ⚠️ **Überholt durch [D-096](#)** — was hier steht, gilt **nicht mehr unverändert**; die geltende Fassung steht dort. *Welcher Teil fiel, sagt die Zeile weiter unten.* ~~The preview shows both modes and is the one place the caller does not state one.~~ **`read_only` removes the input, not the field** — a read-only attribute still appears in a form as a locked control. That half stands and is confirmed by the owner. | **half superseded by [D-096](#)** — the preview turned out to need no exception | [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md) | — |

| D-096 | 2026-08-22 | **The preview needs no special arrangement — it calls render twice**, once editable and once not. Nothing in the contract knows about previewing, nothing branches on *am I a preview*, and the mode is always supplied by the caller. Supersedes the exception in [D-095](#). **This also closes [OQ-034](91-open-questions.md): the preview is a caller, not a renderer**, so [R1](30-renderer.md) keeps no exception. | `agreed` | [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md) | closes [OQ-034](91-open-questions.md) |
| D-097 | 2026-08-22 | ~~A multi-valued edge names a container renderer, chosen **at the edge** because that is where multiplicity lives.~~ | **corrected by [D-098](#)** — that invented a special case | [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md) | — |

| D-098 | 2026-08-22 | **A container renderer is chosen like every other renderer. Corrects [D-097](#).** Table, form, compact row and compact column follow the ordinary chain — **default on the node, override at the use site** ([D-015](#), [R41](30-renderer.md)). A model such as `Bauteilliste` may state *draw me as a table* on the **node**, and a particular use may say otherwise. My earlier wording — *the container is chosen at the edge, because multiplicity lives there* — quietly invented a special case. **What multiplicity actually constrains is not where the renderer is chosen but which are eligible:** an edge with `1..*` needs a renderer that can draw many, so `supports()` also declares one-or-many and the choice list offers only renderers that fit — [D-056](#) again. A cell still never inherits the container's renderer ([D-093](#)). | `agreed` | [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md) | — |

| D-099 | 2026-08-22 | **A second eligibility rule: grouping renderers are not offered for data-type nodes**, which have their own. They apply to nodes that do not inherit from a data type — a spinner belongs to an integer, a table to something with structure, and offering *render as a table* for `Integer` is noise in a list [D-056](#) says should hold only real choices. **This needs a way to know what a data type is, and the proposal is that the data-type root is *declared*, not hard-coded** — an installation-level setting naming the branch(es), which [D-079](#) already provides a place for. Configuration rather than a magic node name, which the code standard forbids. **Same classification [OQ-048](91-open-questions.md) needs**, so both should be answered together. | `agreed` (the declared-root part is a proposal) | [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md) | bears on [OQ-048](91-open-questions.md) |

| D-100 | 2026-08-22 | **The descent stops in two different ways, and they are not the same event.** A **cycle** stop means *you have already seen this* — nothing is missing, so a **reference** to where the node was already drawn is complete information. A **depth-limit** stop means *there is more here and I stopped* — something the model holds is being withheld, so it needs a **warning** as well as a reference. **Who sees the warning depends on the level:** in admin and preview it is shown loudly, where it can be acted on; on the frontend the reference is graceful and the overrun is **recorded for the author**, since a visitor gains nothing from it and can do nothing about it. **Rules:** cycles are detected, not forbidden (mutual references are ordinary modelling) — forbidden only for **inheritance** and **composition** ⚠️ *my call*; visited identities are remembered; a stop draws a reference, or a placeholder where the level cannot carry links; the depth limit is a **setting with a default**, not a constant, so the preview may be stricter than the frontend. **One guard serves both the render descent and the calculation walk** ([D-045](#)). Principle: **a stopped descent is an event, not a nothing.** | `agreed` (confirmed by the owner 2026-08-23) | [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md), [60 Calculation](60-calculation.md) | closes [OQ-019](91-open-questions.md) |

| D-101 | 2026-08-22 | **The preview is the pre-flight check — its third and most important job.** Beside *see the effect of a setting* ([R21–R23](30-renderer.md)) and *render twice* ([D-096](#)), the preview is **where rendering problems surface before a visitor meets them**: depth exceeded, no renderer found for a type, a label missing in this locale, a reference resolving to nothing. **None of these stop anything** — the renderer carries on and produces something — which is precisely why they need a place to appear, since a page that renders *almost* right, silently, is the hardest fault to notice. **Consequence: the preview previews a *level*.** To warn about what the frontend will do it must render at the frontend's settings; its own stricter limit would raise false alarms and a looser one would miss real ones. So the author chooses which level is being previewed ([R8](30-renderer.md)) — which the preview arguably needs anyway, since the same model may legitimately look different in each. | `agreed` | [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md) | refines [D-100](#) |

| D-102 | 2026-08-22 | **Modelling guidance: a symmetric relationship is better modelled as membership in a group than as pairwise links.** *Alternative to*, *similar to*, *belongs with* — pairwise links must be maintained in both directions and drift out of sync; membership is stated once and is symmetric by construction. Raised by the owner as a possible way to model replacement products. **It does not remove the cycle** — `Bauteil → Austauschgruppe → Bauteil` is one hop longer, and making it acyclic would stop the group listing its members, which was the point — so the loop guard of [D-100](#) stands regardless. | `agreed` | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md), [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md) | — |

| D-103 | 2026-08-22 | **Depth belongs to the rendering, not to the node. The caller decides and the context carries it.** A Gutenberg **block instance** carries its own depth — a block attribute, not a model setting, since a block is neither node nor edge — and it reaches the renderer through the `RenderContext` like every other circumstance. Resolution order: what the **caller** passes → the **level** default (frontend / block / admin) → the **installation** setting ([D-079](#)). Two blocks may show the same parts list two and five levels deep on one page, and both are right. A node-level cap would be possible but nobody has asked for it. The previous project already had per-block depth — a harvest confirmation, not an inheritance. | `agreed` | [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md) | — |
| D-104 | 2026-08-22 | **A depth limit is a rendering concern only. Calculations must never be truncated.** A truncated rendering shows **less than the truth** and says so ([D-100](#)); a truncated sum **states an untruth**, in the same typeface as a correct number, with nothing about it looking unfinished. The [calculation walk](60-calculation.md) therefore needs the **cycle** guard — without it it never terminates — but carries **no depth cap**. If a calculation cannot complete for any reason its result is **not a number**: it is marked *not computable*, and every renderer shows that rather than a value. | `agreed` | [60 Calculation](60-calculation.md), [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md) | opens [OQ-062](91-open-questions.md) |

| D-105 | 2026-08-22 | **A reference renderer: it draws the target's label and a link, and does not descend.** Proposed by the owner as a manual stop at an aggregation, and it does three things. **(1) It bounds the load, not only the display** — [D-014](#)'s batched pass now has a declared boundary, so it becomes finite **by design** instead of by depth guessing; nothing behind a reference is fetched. **(2) It expresses intent**, so the cycle guard moves from *the thing that saves us* to *the thing that catches a mistake*; in the preview the automatic stops are marked as such ([D-101](#)) while the deliberate one is not. **(3) Composition expands by default, aggregation references by default** — a composed part belongs to its whole, an aggregated target is independent ([C13](10-domain-core.md)), so the kind carries its own natural presentation as a default renderer per kind, overridable by the ordinary chain ([D-098](#)). Since aggregation is the only kind that can cycle, this makes cycles rare in practice without making them impossible. **Convergence:** a reference renderer *is* the old `display_node_name`, reworked by [D-044](#), reached here from an entirely different direction. | `agreed` | [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md) | harvest 01 B5 |

| D-106 | 2026-08-22 | **Display and input are independent, and the display is not binary. Clarifies [D-105](#).** *Not descending* never meant *not editable*: an aggregation in edit mode shows a **chooser** ([D-035](#)) whatever renderer draws it afterwards, so a part in a parts list is selected and filled in exactly as expected. And the display has three settings, not two — **reference** (label and link) · **summary** (a few of the target's attributes, what a parts-list row wants) · **expand** (the whole target) — all ordinary renderer choices on the edge ([D-098](#)). The **reference stays the default** because it is the only one that loads nothing beyond itself, so an author opts *into* fetching more rather than having to notice they should opt out; if most aggregations turn out to want a summary, changing the default costs nothing. | `agreed` | [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md) | — |

| D-107 | 2026-08-22 | ⚠️ **Überholt durch [D-108](#)** — was hier steht, gilt **nicht mehr unverändert**; die geltende Fassung steht dort. *Welcher Teil fiel, sagt die Zeile weiter unten.* **A chooser is a renderer** ([OQ-038](91-open-questions.md) closed). It renders an identity, takes a context, returns markup, and gets its **branch node** and **default node** ([D-035](#)) through that context. **The popup is render-conform:** the renderer produces markup **plus metadata** (branch, default, allow-list, multiplicity) and **one generic JavaScript component** supplies the interaction — which is [D-021](#) exactly, not an exception to it. So **a renderer may have a behaviour partner, and the partner is generic**; no node type ever needs its own JavaScript. ~~Inline versus popup is a setting.~~ → **superseded on that point by [D-108](#): two separate renderers.** | `agreed`, inline/popup part superseded by [D-108](#) | [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md) | closes [OQ-038](91-open-questions.md) |

| D-108 | 2026-08-22 | **Inline and popup are two separate chooser renderers, not one with a setting. Supersedes that part of [D-107](#).** An ordinary application of [D-018](#) — completely different presentation, separate renderers — with **inline as the default renderer**. **Both objections I raised against it were wrong:** *two entries in the choice list for one logical thing* is equally true of field, spinner and slider and was accepted there; and *an installation-wide preference is awkward* fails because [D-079](#) puts the installation at the head of the settings chain and the renderer choice **is** a setting. The *decided on use rather than on the rule* note is withdrawn — the rule was sound. | `agreed` | [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md) | — |
| D-109 | 2026-08-22 | **List or tree is derived from the branch, not chosen.** One level → a selection list; several levels → a tree view. Not a third renderer and not a setting — a tree control for a flat set is furniture with nothing to do, which is [D-056](#) again. This is also why an enumerated choice list never needed a type of its own: it is an attribute whose branch happens to be one level deep ([D-041](#)). | `agreed` | [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md) | — |
| D-110 | 2026-08-22 | **The branch root is excluded from the choice by default, with a setting to include it.** `Gewicht.einheit → Masseneinheit` offers *Gramm* and *Kilogramm*, not *Masseneinheit*. But a department chooser rooted at the company may legitimately allow the company, so the exclusion is a **rule with an escape**, not an absolute — one boolean sitting at its default almost everywhere, which satisfies [D-050](#) because it is a default and not a question. ⚠️ **Deriving it was rejected:** *a node with children is a category, a leaf is a choice* works for units and breaks for organisational units, where a department with sub-departments is still a valid answer. | `agreed` | [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md) | — |

| D-111 | 2026-08-22 | **The multi-step input, and the create form is the search field.** Step one is the chooser ([D-035](#), [D-108](#)), re-openable to change the selection; step two is the ordinary editor ([D-029](#)) rendering the chosen type's attributes, metadata-driven so no type needs its own JavaScript ([D-021](#)). **At step two there are two paths — search what exists, or enter new — and the owner's rule is that one must always check first.** Left as an instruction to the user that rule is broken every busy afternoon, so: **as the author types the identifying values, matching existing records appear beneath, and *create new* is offered when nothing matches or as an explicit second action, never as the only visible path.** The check then happens because it cannot be skipped. Same spirit as [D-050](#). | `agreed` | [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md) | opens [OQ-063](91-open-questions.md) |

| D-112 | 2026-08-22 | **The shown fields are the searched fields.** The columns a summary renderer already chooses ([D-106](#)) do a second job: they are what a person reads to recognise a record, so they are what the duplicate search matches. **No new setting for the common case.** Because shown fields are per use site, a type may additionally **declare its own identifying set** that applies wherever it is used — the default covers the common case, the declaration exists where consistency genuinely matters, such as an article number. **Matching is *contains*** — a wildcard before and after — so `10 kilo` finds `10 kilo` and `BC 547 B` can find `BC547B`. **Hard identity remains the `id`** ([D-055](#)); this is soft identity and it only ever warns. | `agreed` | [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md) | answers most of [OQ-063](91-open-questions.md) |
| D-113 | 2026-08-22 | **Inline for searching, popup for creating.** Typing and picking while filling a row keeps the flow, and a popup per row would be tedious; a new record with fifteen attributes cannot live in a row. [D-032](#) applies, so it is a configured default rather than an absolute — a place may insist on the popup. | `agreed` | [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md) | — |

| D-114 | 2026-08-22 | **An attribute may carry a `unique` setting — enforced, not advisory.** Two mechanisms of different strength: **identifying** fields make the duplicate search **warn** ([D-112](#)); `unique` makes the save **refuse**. **Uniqueness comes out of inheritance for free:** an attribute is an edge and a subtype inherits that edge rather than getting its own, so a check on `(edge_id, value)` covers every record of every subtype at once — *unique among all Bauteile, including the specialised ones*, without anyone designing it. **Enforced in two layers** like [D-074](#): the validator is what a person meets, the index is the last line against concurrency, and a user never sees a database error. The index lives in the derived search structure of [OQ-064](91-open-questions.md) rather than on `record_values`, since a filtered index is unavailable. **Empty does not participate.** **And the best response to a violation is not an error** — [V9](00-vision-and-scope.md)'s offered correction is the whole point: *this number belongs to «Widerstand 10k 0805», did you mean that one?* with the action to select it. **A uniqueness violation is the duplicate detection succeeding.** | `agreed` | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md), [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md) | closes [OQ-063](91-open-questions.md) |

| D-115 | 2026-08-22 | **`unique` may name a group, forming a composite constraint — and it is not called *primary key*.** Attributes sharing a group name are unique **together** (manufacturer plus type designation); an attribute with `unique` and no group is unique on its own, and there may be several such constraints (article number, EAN). **The term *primary key* is refused** because the primary key is the **`id`** ([D-055](#)): an article number is human-meaningful, correctable, and a record survives changing it, which an `id` is not and does not — using one word for both re-opens the hard/soft identity distinction that took effort to establish. `unique` also permits several, where *primary key* implies one. **The duplicate check follows from it** and needs nothing extra: the search matches the shown fields ([D-112](#)), and where one is unique the warning becomes a refusal with an offered correction ([D-114](#)). | `agreed` | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md), [01 Glossary](01-glossary.md) | — |

| D-116 | 2026-08-22 | **A prefix is a node, not an enum — and the general rule is that a fixed value which might one day carry properties is a node.** By the criterion of [D-085](#): the engine multiplies by a prefix's factor and never branches on *which* prefix, so the meaning belongs to the value, not the engine. **The owner's history says the same from experience:** the previous project had an enum type, dropped once fixed values turned out to carry further properties — and a prefix already proves it, having looked like a name and turned out to need a multiplier. Prefixes are therefore *content determined at modelling time* ([D-048](#)), the same class as base units, currencies and shapes. | `agreed` | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md) | closes [OQ-041](91-open-questions.md) |
| D-117 | 2026-08-22 | **What a structure *looks like* is a renderer; what it *is* is a node with attributes.** This sorts the old `Complex Datatypes` branch, which held two different things: `Unit type` and `quantity › Preis` are **genuine composed types** ([C44](10-domain-core.md)), while `set` and `table` are **container renderers** ([D-097](#)) and the dropped `enum` is **an attribute whose branch is one level deep**, drawn as a list ([D-109](#)). Two of the three dissolve into decisions already taken, and none of them needed to be a type. | `agreed` | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md), [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md) | harvest 01 |

| D-118 | 2026-08-22 | **A node lays its attributes out in four groups, the same on every level.** **1** read-only values — context rather than input, visible in the admin by default and hidden in the frontend, `hide` overriding either way; **2** ordinary fields; **3** booleans **collected**, flowing along a row and wrapping, column-aligned; **4** multi-valued attributes last. Reasons, recorded so the rule is not improved away: a table is visually heavy and breaks the reading flow, one switch per row wastes the row, and the multi-valued block may be arbitrarily tall so the eye should settle there. **`position` orders *within* a group** ([D-082](#)), so the layout stays tidy and the sequence stays the author's — and per [D-032](#) the grouping is a configured default that a renderer or use site may switch to strict author order, for the case where a boolean genuinely belongs between two text fields. | `agreed` | [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md) | — |

| D-119 | 2026-08-22 | **A base scaffold ships and is imported once; afterwards it is ordinary authored content.** Contents: simple data types, base units, a few currencies, the general settings — **everything beyond that is authored** ([V7](00-vision-and-scope.md)). **The seed is a backup export in the format of [D-058](#) and the import runs through the conflict resolver of [D-059](#)** — no new machinery. Updates **offer** new items additively and **never overwrite**, so a plugin update cannot undo an author's edit. Further packs (imperial, domain-specific) are separate importable files in the same format. | `agreed` | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md), [70 Migration](70-migration.md) | closes [OQ-043](91-open-questions.md) |
| D-120 | 2026-08-22 | **A binding is a named slot in the installation configuration that points at a node — and nothing else.** The engine and the configuration ask for the slot; **nothing anywhere names a node id or a node name**. This makes [C94](10-domain-core.md) free: ids may shift on insert or differ between installations, because the binding absorbs it. **A binding carries no renderer and no defaults** — the node already does ([D-015](#), [D-098](#)), and a second configuration place competing with the first is the duplication this concept keeps refusing. It also turns two earlier proposals into a mechanism: the *declared data-type root* ([D-099](#), [OQ-048](91-open-questions.md)) **is** a binding, and bindings live on the installation identity ([D-079](#)). **Division of labour with machine keys:** bindings for **roots and singletons** (`datatype.integer`, `unit.root`), a `unique` machine key ([D-115](#)) for **members of a set** (the ISO code on every currency) — one would not create a hundred and eighty bindings for currencies. | `agreed` | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md), [50 Persistence](50-wordpress-persistence.md) | old `class-catalog-bindings.php` used the same word |

| D-121 | 2026-08-22 | ~~Deletion protection is derived from references, not from a *template* flag.~~ The reference half stands and is kept by [D-122](#); the rejection of a marker was wrong. | **corrected by [D-122](#)** | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md) | — |

| D-122 | 2026-08-22 | ⚠️ **Überholt durch [D-194](#)** — was hier steht, gilt **nicht mehr unverändert**; die geltende Fassung steht dort. *Welcher Teil fiel, sagt die Zeile weiter unten.* **Framework types are marked and undeletable; the reference check stands beside it. Corrects [D-121](#).** I argued protection should be *derived* from references because derived is more precise — **precision was the wrong goal.** A binding refusal says *a binding points at this*, a technicality that explains nothing; the user needs to hear *this is a framework type*. And the two are not alternatives: the **marker** protects the types the engine is built on, the **reference check** protects anything else something points at. A **developer flag** lifts the marker — protection by default, deliberately liftable, [D-032](#) with friction added on purpose so a normal author never meets the question. ⚠️ **Das Kennzeichen ist ersetzt** durch [D-194](#); der Rest gilt. | `agreed` | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md) | old project had both |
| D-123 | 2026-08-22 | **Deletion is two-stage: park, then purge.** Deleted nodes and relations are marked deleted and parked under the root; only from there are they removed for good. This makes the whole deletion question gentler — a reference to a parked node **does not dangle**, conflicts ([D-062](#)) can be resolved after parking rather than in a dialog blocking the delete, and the **purge** is the irreversible moment where the reference check applies in full. | `agreed` | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md) | old `class-trash.php` |
| D-124 | 2026-08-22 | **Moving a node is referentially free and semantically a model change.** Nothing resolves on position ([D-055](#), [D-120](#)), so no reference breaks — but moving between branches changes the node's **inheritance** and therefore which attributes it has ([D-041](#)). So a move runs the same check as any other model change: does existing data still fit ([D-037](#))? | `agreed` | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md) | opens [OQ-066](91-open-questions.md) |

| D-125 | 2026-08-22 | **Deleting a referenced node parks every edge that points at it, and that is *an attribute was deleted* for each owning node** — already covered by [M17](70-migration.md) and [D-063](#): warn at the moment of the change, the records still hold the field, the author decides delete or transfer. Nothing new for the consequence; what changes is the weight of the moment. | `agreed` | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md) | — |
| D-126 | 2026-08-22 | **The confirmation names the consequences and requires an act that cannot be reflexive.** Not *are you sure* but *what exactly falls* — which attributes leave which nodes, and how many records hold a value for each — and then an act like typing the node's name rather than a button sitting where *cancel* usually is. A dialog whose safest answer is one click away will be clicked. [V9](00-vision-and-scope.md)'s spirit: the system knows what will happen, so it says so instead of asking a person to imagine it. | `agreed` | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md), [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md) | — |
| D-127 | 2026-08-22 | **A trash entry is one deletion event, with everything that fell with it — and restore puts back the whole event.** If parking cascades from a node to the edges pointing at it, restoring must cascade too; otherwise a restore returns a node whose attributes stayed in the trash and nobody can reason about the result. | `agreed` | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md) | — |
| D-128 | 2026-08-22 | **Three surfaces for a deletion, and they are different.** The **parked node** is listed in the trash with what fell with it. The **parked attribute** is **hidden by default** in its owning node — a model full of ghost attributes is unreadable — one *show deleted* toggle away, greyed, labelled *deleted with «X»*, with a restore action. The **orphaned values** in records appear in the **conflict list** ([D-054](#)), because that is where they get resolved.
