Reasoning: the modelling view answers *what does this node look like now*, and a deleted attribute is not part of that answer — but it must not become invisible either. | `agreed` (proposal answering C105) | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md), [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md) | — |

| D-129 | 2026-08-22 | **A demo pack ships beside the scaffold: an example tree with data, optional and importable.** Requested by the owner so an author can see how things are done and try them out. **No new machinery** — it is a pack in the sense of [D-119](#), the same export format, the same importer. Three things now ship and they are different: the **scaffold** (framework essentials, required, [D-119](#)) · the **demo pack** (a worked example model *with* data, optional) · **test-data records** (the `is_test` flag driving previews, [D-028](#)). The demo pack doubles as a **regression fixture**: if it can be imported, built and rendered, the engine works — which is what the harvested Fallstudie tree already is. | `agreed` | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md), [70 Migration](70-migration.md), [95 Roadmap](95-roadmap.md) | old `class-demo-data.php`, `reset-demo-tree.php` |

| D-130 | 2026-08-22 | **The expression is a structured tree, built with a picker rather than typed.** Operations and operands, from a small closed set: **arithmetic** (`+ − × ÷`) and **aggregates** over a collection (`sum`, `avg`, `min`, `max`, `count`). Neither pole worked alone — a picked operation over one field cannot express `menge × einzelpreis`, and a free formula language needs a parser, evaluation safety and error messages a **model author** could act on. **What the tree buys:** no parser and no syntax errors; operands picked from real attributes and stored as **edge ids** ([D-045](#)) so renaming and moving leave them intact; type checking while building, so the error never comes into being; the **dependency graph *is* the tree**, so D-045's invalidation index falls out instead of being parsed; and scalar-versus-aggregate is checkable from the multiplicity along the path. **A formula field may later be a second way to author the same structure** — typed, parsed, stored as the tree: *the structure is the truth, text is an input method* — so this decision closes nothing off. **Hard cases arrive as a registered calculation strategy** ([D-036](#)), the same mechanism as renderers, converters and validators: *simple things structurally, hard things as a registered strategy*. **Stored serialised in the setting**, since nothing queries inside an expression. | `agreed` | [60 Calculation](60-calculation.md) | closes [OQ-047](91-open-questions.md) |

| D-131 | 2026-08-22 | **A value reference resolves either to a **node** or to a **record**, and the target type's placement decides which.** Following [D-048](#): a target type whose content is determined **at modelling time** (`Basiseinheit` → `Gramm`) yields a **node** reference; one determined **at input time** (`Bauteil` → a specific part) yields a **record** reference. **This is what the bindings are actually for** — not *where data may be entered*, which they do not answer. | `agreed` | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md), [50 Persistence](50-wordpress-persistence.md) | corrects the framing of [OQ-048](91-open-questions.md) |
| D-132 | 2026-08-22 | **Standalone-versus-composed applies only to nodes whose instances are records.** A node that is *modelling-time content* — `Gramm` — is not in that category at all. ⚠️ **The derivation from incoming edges is demoted by [D-135](#)/[D-138](#):** placement under `Kompositionen` now **declares** it, visibly and changeably, so the derivation is no longer the truth — it **suggests** where the tool should put a newly created node ([D-136](#)). *The derivation suggests, the placement declares.* Also corrected: a first version mixed model-level edges with data-level value references, listing `Gramm` as an aggregation target when the edge points at `Basiseinheit` and `Gramm` is a *value*. | `agreed`, derivation demoted | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md) | closes [OQ-048](91-open-questions.md) |

| D-133 | 2026-08-22 | ⚠️ **Überholt durch [D-232](#)** — was hier steht, gilt **nicht mehr unverändert**; die geltende Fassung steht dort. *Welcher Teil fiel, sagt die Zeile weiter unten.* **Where a value is stored follows from the relation kind and the multiplicity. Supersedes the storage half of [D-083](#).** The owner rejected *every composed value is a record of its own* — **a price is not a record**. The rule uses only what already exists: **aggregation** → stored **externally**, a reference to a record or, for modelling-time content, to a node ([D-131](#)); **composition with multiplicity 1** → stored **internally** in the owning record, addressed by path; **composition with multiplicity > 1** → **own records**, owned and cascade-deleted with the whole ([C12](10-domain-core.md)). The boundary is principled rather than chosen: *what you can have several of needs its own identity; what you have exactly one of does not* — a position is a thing in a list, a price is a property of a position. **Deduplicating equal values was refused separately:** two positions at 20 € do not share a price, they each have one, and changing one must not change the other — equal is not the same. | **superseded by [D-232](#)** | [50 Persistence](50-wordpress-persistence.md), [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md) | — |
| D-134 | 2026-08-22 | **`record_values` keys on a path, with the last edge kept in `edge_id`.** Required by [D-133](#)'s flattening. It preserves and improves the search story of [D-070](#): `WHERE edge_id = wert AND value_decimal > 1000` finds every price over a thousand wherever it sits, and adding `path` narrows it to one particular attribute. **Honest cost: changing a multiplicity from 1 to `1..*` becomes a storage migration**, flattened values becoming records — real, but honest rather than arbitrary, since the change alters what the thing *is*, from a property to a list of things, and [D-054](#)'s resolver exists for such changes. In exchange the record tree becomes much shallower, helping loading, rendering and calculation alike. | `agreed` | [50 Persistence](50-wordpress-persistence.md) | — |

| D-135 | 2026-08-22 | **Composed-only nodes live under a `Kompositionen` node. Corrects [D-017](#).** *Beneath its whole* was wrong: the tree is **inheritance only** ([V3](00-vision-and-scope.md)), so hanging `Position` under `Bauteilliste` would make a position **inherit** the parts list. ⚠️ **And it is not a *container*** — corrected by the owner: it is an ordinary node that **declares** something, and inheritance passes that declaration to its children. What it declares: **only composition edges may point at me**. That makes the self-check a real assurance rather than a naming convention — an aggregation pointing at a descendant of `Kompositionen` is a **contradiction in the model**. **Locality is not lost** — the model view shows the composed structure inline at its owner by following the edge, which is presentation, not structure. | `agreed` | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md) | the owner caught both errors |
| D-136 | 2026-08-22 | **A multi-valued composition creates its node automatically.** Setting a multiplicity above 1 on a structure makes the tool create the node under `Kompositionen`, named from owner and attribute (`Bauteilliste-Position`) and renameable. Duplicate names are no problem — base names are explicitly not unique ([D-022](#)) and the nodes are told apart by the edge pointing at them. **In the author's head: one model with a repeating group. In the tree: a node under `Kompositionen`. Shown: at the parts list, where it belongs.** | `agreed` | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md), [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md) | — |
| D-137 | 2026-08-22 | **Converting between the kinds is a move, with one asymmetry.** **Composition → aggregation:** move the node from `Kompositionen` to the model branch; **free when multiplicity is above 1** (the data are already separate records, [D-133](#)) and a **migration when multiplicity is 1** (embedded path rows become records). **Aggregation → composition** needs **two** checks, not one: at **model level** only one edge may point at it, and at **data level** each target record must be referenced by **at most one** owner record. The second is sharper and can fail while the first holds — `Position.artikel → Bauteil` is one edge, but one *Widerstand 10k* record hangs on five hundred positions. Converting that is not a conversion but a **duplication**, which the resolver may offer explicitly and must never do silently. **And placement follows the kind, so the tree is self-checking:** a node under `Kompositionen` that an aggregation points at is a detectable inconsistency. | `agreed` | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md), [70 Migration](70-migration.md) | — |

| D-138 | 2026-08-22 | **The `Kompositionen` node gets a binding, like every other root the engine must find.** Without one the engine could only locate it by name, which the code standard forbids ([D-120](#)). The model root is bound the same way, so both ends of [D-137](#)'s move are named slots rather than conventions — and moving or renaming either branch changes nothing. | `agreed` | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md) | — |

| D-139 | 2026-08-22 | **What has data of its own is decided by placement, visibly.** A node lying **neither in the model branch nor in the composition branch has no data of its own** — its values live in the model that uses it. A composition edge may point at a node under `Kompositionen` **or** at a data type that has no data. **This agrees with [D-133](#)** for every case; where they disagree — a multiplicity above 1 set on a **data type** — the resolution is [D-136](#)'s automatic creation: a node under `Kompositionen` **inheriting from** the data type (`Bauteil-Preisverlauf` from `Preis`), so the shared type is untouched and other uses notice nothing. Inheritance used for a **use-site-specific variety of a shared type**, the same movement as [D-105](#). | `agreed` | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md), [50 Persistence](50-wordpress-persistence.md) | — |
| D-140 | 2026-08-22 | **An operand may be a backward path, and a backward aggregate is computed on read, never materialised.** *Who points at me* — the average purchase price of a part across all order lines. The **infrastructure already exists**: it is [D-070](#)'s reverse query, indexed; only the expression form was missing. **It is not materialised because invalidation fans out** — a new order line would touch a computed field on a part and thence every parts list using it, and [D-072](#) would turn every order write into a cascade. So: **forward calculations materialise and are searchable; backward aggregates are read-time and are not** — a deliberate exception to D-072, since such a figure is read rather than filtered on. Turning materialisation on for one is an explicit choice that accepts the cascade. | `agreed` | [60 Calculation](60-calculation.md) | extends [D-130](#); answers the reach half of [OQ-045](91-open-questions.md) |
| D-141 | 2026-08-22 | **Durability is a lifecycle question, not a storage-shape question.** A composed price dies with its parts list, and that is correct — the prices of a deleted parts list are not history. Where a document must survive, it is **not deleted**: [D-123](#)'s park-and-purge already separates preserving from destroying, and purging an order would simply be forbidden. **How order prices are modelled therefore already follows from [D-065](#):** a list price *describes* and tracks; a price in an order line *was agreed* and freezes, with its exchange rate ([D-064](#)). Both are prices on the same part and behave oppositely because they are different statements. | `agreed` | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md), [70 Migration](70-migration.md) | forward note for the ordering stage |

| D-142 | 2026-08-22 | **Contagion: a calculation that depends on a backward aggregate is itself computed on read, and is therefore not searchable.** Raised by the owner's own composition `Position.preis = Bauteil.durchschnittspreis × menge` — a **forward** calculation whose operand is a **backward** aggregate. Materialising it would leave the stored value stale as soon as an order was added anywhere, with **nothing to invalidate it**, since backward aggregates are not tracked ([D-140](#)) — exactly the wrong search result [D-072](#) exists to prevent. **The escape is to materialise the cause, not the symptom:** anyone needing `gesamtpreis` searchable turns materialisation on at the **average price**, and everything downstream is materialised and correct, with the cascade on order writes as the consciously paid price. This puts the cost where it can be understood — *you want to search on it, so this has to be tracked* — rather than leaving someone wondering why a search is odd. | `agreed` | [60 Calculation](60-calculation.md) | — |

| D-143 | 2026-08-22 | **A computed value has three states, not two — and freezing dissolves the contagion problem.** **live forward** (computed on write, materialised, searchable, cascades forward) · **live backward** (computed on read, not searchable, no cascade, [D-140](#)) · **frozen** (computed **once, on request**, stored, **searchable**, **no cascade**). A frozen value has no running dependency — it is a stored number that was computed once — so [D-142](#)'s contagion does not apply and `gesamtpreis` becomes searchable again even though the average price behind it is backward. The owner's reason is the decisive one: *it is only an approximation*, and **propagating an approximation live through an archive costs much and gains nothing**. | `agreed` | [60 Calculation](60-calculation.md) | resolves the tension in [D-142](#) |
| D-144 | 2026-08-22 | **Recalculation is an event, and staleness is information.** An explicit *recalculate* **shows what will change before applying it** — *12 of 100 positions change · Widerstand 10k 0,043 → 0,051 €* — because a silent refresh of a hundred line prices is alarming; the same shape as the conflict resolver. A **frozen value carries a timestamp**, so nobody has to guess how old it is. And the current figure is cheap to compute on read ([D-140](#)), so a list can carry a **hint**: *3 positions differ by more than 10% from current prices* — turning *I think this is out of date* into information. | `agreed` | [60 Calculation](60-calculation.md), [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md) | — |
| D-145 | 2026-08-22 | **A valuation method is a registered calculation strategy, and several valuations coexist as separate named attributes.** The average is one of a family — moving average, FIFO, value of stock on hand, replacement cost — and none is right everywhere; the owner's own rule (*use the stock value, fall back to what I would pay to reorder*) is a real one. Which applies is a **setting** with the usual default and override chain, and the strategy mechanism is the one already used for renderers, converters and validators ([D-130](#)). **Two constraints:** each method is only as good as its data — average needs order lines, FIFO needs receipts with quantity and date, stock value needs inventory — so **start with the average because it is what the data supports**; and these are **different numbers for different purposes, not competitors**, so a part carries `einstandspreis`, `wiederbeschaffungspreis`, `listenpreis` side by side rather than one field `preis` that people argue over. | `agreed` | [60 Calculation](60-calculation.md), [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md) | — |
| D-146 | 2026-08-22 | **A tracking list recalculates on save; a frozen list never does.** Saving is the natural moment — the list was touched anyway, a change is expected, and afterwards it holds a real assurance: *these prices are from the last time someone worked on this*. A frozen list must **not** recalculate on save, or *frozen* means nothing. **Both behaviours follow from one setting** ([D-065](#)), and no new concept is needed. Even the tracking case **reports afterwards** — *12 prices updated* — non-blocking but visible, since correcting a typo should not move a hundred numbers unnoticed. | `agreed` | [60 Calculation](60-calculation.md) | — |

| D-147 | 2026-08-22 | **A computed value handles a missing input in one of three modes, set per attribute.** **strict** — anything missing makes the whole not computable; **partial** *(default)* — compute what is available and **mark the result**: *1 240 € · 3 positions not computable*; **substitute** — use the attribute's **default**, which already inherits ([D-030](#), [D-015](#)), so a class estimate falls out of the hierarchy with no new mechanism. **Treating a missing input as zero is explicitly not an option** — [D-104](#) forbids it, and it is recorded as rejected so nobody adds it later for convenience. **Marking happens in both places, and they say different things:** at the **value** it says *here is the cause*, for whoever will fix it; at the **aggregate** it says *this number is incomplete*, for whoever **uses** it — and that person may never see the position. Without the second, an incomplete figure travels onward as if it were complete. Three distinguishable states at a value: computed · not computable (`—` with a reason) · estimated (figure shown, marked as an estimate). | `agreed` | [60 Calculation](60-calculation.md), [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md) | closes [OQ-062](91-open-questions.md) |
| D-148 | 2026-08-22 | **Converters are a few *kinds* parameterised by data, not one hard-coded class per case.** The engine branches on the **form** of the mapping, never on its content — the same criterion as [D-085](#). Four forms cover the cases raised: **lookup** (a table, value ↔ display: AWG ↔ mm², digit ↔ colour) · **threshold** (bounds → output: a **traffic light**, tolerance classes, size charts) · **scale** (factor and unit: prefixes, the capacitor code) · **format** (a pattern: dates, part numbers). A traffic light is therefore **data an author enters**, not work for a developer. **A threshold mapping is readable backwards**, so *show me all the red ones* becomes a range query on the real value — indexed, exact, and nothing can go stale, which is why no derived field is needed. Genuine algorithms remain available as a **registered strategy** ([D-130](#)), as the exception rather than the rule. | `agreed` | [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md) | — |
| D-149 | 2026-08-22 | **Notation is not structure.** If a second form carries nothing the first lacks, it is a **writing of the same value** — a converter plus a renderer, **never a second field**, since two stored values can drift apart. **One attribute, one stored value, one renderer with two coupled controls:** the renderer uses the converter twice, once to display the code and once to parse input from the code side, and wherever the user types, the canonical value is what is stored. The pattern is general — a hex field beside a colour picker, a text field beside a calendar, decimal degrees beside degrees-minutes-seconds. **And a renderer that spans several attributes is a node renderer, not an attribute renderer** ([D-091](#)). | `agreed` | [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md) | — |
| D-150 | 2026-08-22 | **`Widerstandswert` is a composed type — value plus tolerance — and tolerance defaults to 10%.** This keeps the colour code **one notation of one thing** ([D-149](#)), so it stays an attribute renderer rather than needing to span attributes. **Staging, weighted by where the benefit lies:** the **resistor** gets a **renderer** first — the value is in the *display*, the bands are visual and awkward to type; the **capacitor** gets a **converter** first — the value is in the *input*, since `104` is printed on the part and someone wants to type it into a search and find it ([D-076](#) makes the converter run on the way in). Later the resistor gains a converter too, for the workshop case of reading bands off a part in hand — the renderer is unaffected. | `agreed` | [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md), [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md) | — |

| D-151 | 2026-08-22 | **Label roles are nodes: a seeded base set, extensible by the author.** They are modelling-time content ([D-048](#)) under their own branch, and `labels.role` references them — which buys a **picker** instead of free text, **no typo roles** pointing nowhere, and lets a role **carry properties of its own**. Possible because [D-044](#) already made the role a **setting** a renderer reads rather than a constant it hard-codes, so the name merely flows through and may be data. **`long` is mandatory** — the fallback chain of [D-020](#) ends `<role>` → `long` → `node.name` and breaks without it. **The long description doubles as the tooltip**, so no separate `tooltip` role is needed. Base set: `long`, `form`, `table`, `symbol` — ~~plus `plural`~~ → **withdrawn by [D-153](#)**: number is a dimension, not a role. Further roles (`abbreviation`, `placeholder`, `help`) are additions rather than decisions to get right now. | `agreed` | [40 I18n](40-i18n.md) | closes [OQ-028](91-open-questions.md); the seed had a fifth role, `short`, lost along the way |
| D-152 | 2026-08-22 | **Length hints are settings on the role node.** `symbol ≤ 3`, `form ≤ 15` from the seed sketch become ordinary settings on the role, editable and inheritable, with no new mechanism — a direct consequence of roles being nodes ([D-151](#)). **They are advisory by default**, since a limit set from German will bite in Finnish: the editor warns and the renderer may truncate, but nothing is refused. A role may set the limit to *enforced* where a real constraint exists, such as a fixed-width column in an exported format. | `agreed` | [40 I18n](40-i18n.md) | closes [OQ-029](91-open-questions.md) |

| D-153 | 2026-08-22 | **Number is a column, not a role — `provisional`.** `plural` as a *role* broke as soon as roles carry genuinely different texts: with `table` = *Pos.* and `long` = a description, *the plural of the name* is ambiguous, and the fallback of [D-020](#) would land on a **description** — *no Position, the line of a parts list, found*. **A role says what a text is for; number says which form it is in** — two axes, and the first had the second squeezed into it. So `labels` gains a **`number`** column holding a plural category, **sparsely filled**: nobody enters `symbol · other`, and in practice one row is added. The fallback becomes obvious — **number first, then role**: `form·other` → `form·one` → `long·one` → `node.name`. And languages with more numerus categories need only further allowed values (`few`, `many`), not a redesign. ⚠️ **Doubt, named by the owner:** it is not certain this earns a column at all — the alternative is no mechanism, with authors simply writing *Positionen* into whichever label needs it. **Trigger for revisiting:** when the first list view and the first count are built, check whether a plural was actually needed **on a node** rather than on an edge. | `agreed` — the doubt was resolved by [D-216](#) on 2026-08-23 | [40 I18n](40-i18n.md) | withdraws the `plural` role from [D-151](#) |

| D-154 | 2026-08-22 | **A parked record keeps its `unique` values blocked; purging releases them.** My proposal was the opposite — release on parking, treat a colliding restore as a conflict — and the owner's answer is better because it **removes a whole class of conflict instead of handling it**: with the number still held, **a restore can never collide.** The block carries its reason and the two actions that resolve it: *article number 4711 is held by «Widerstand 10k», deleted on 3 March — restore · purge · use another number* ([V9](00-vision-and-scope.md) again). **And it catches a mistake:** someone who deletes and immediately recreates with the same number usually meant to **restore and edit**; a silent success would produce two records where one was intended, and the block turns the error into a question. **Implementation consequence, noticed here rather than later:** the derived structure holds **two indexes of different scope** — the **search** index excludes parked records, since a deleted part belongs in no chooser, while the **uniqueness** index includes them. After a purge the value is free and the record can be created anew. | `agreed` | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md), [50 Persistence](50-wordpress-persistence.md) | closes [OQ-067](91-open-questions.md) |

| D-155 | 2026-08-22 | **Moving is one rule with two subjects, and it never loses data.** Because an attribute **is** a relation with a stable id ([D-031](#)), moving changes `from_id` while the id stays — and records reference the id, so **values survive**; what changes is **who has the attribute**. **Moving a node** between branches changes its inheritance ([D-124](#)). **Moving an attribute** does the same one level down: **up to the parent** is additive (siblings gain it, their records hold it empty), **down to a child** is removing (siblings lose it, their records hold orphaned values). Both run [D-037](#)'s data-dependent check and warn **before** the change, per [D-063](#), naming what is lost and how many records hold a value. ⚠️ **One caveat on the additive direction:** if the attribute is **mandatory**, siblings must suddenly have it while their records do not — so pushing up can break too, and [D-062](#)'s bulk fill is the answer. | `agreed` | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md), [70 Migration](70-migration.md) | closes [OQ-066](91-open-questions.md) |

| D-156 | 2026-08-22 | **Promotion has everything it needs, because deletion is two-stage — and what it does depends on how many use the target.** [OQ-037](91-open-questions.md) worried that an override knew a setting key but no target node, the information having vanished with the path. **Two-stage deletion removed that worry:** [D-123](#) parks before purging and [D-125](#) parks the referencing edge with it, so at the moment of the decision the edge is still readable — its `to` gives the **type** and its `name` gives the **name**, both editable. *The trash preserves exactly the information the decision needs, which was not planned but falls out.* **What promotion does:** if the target has **one** user, restore the attribute **on the target** — nobody else can be disturbed; if it has **several**, **specialise** — create an inheriting child under `Kompositionen` carrying the attribute and point **this one** use site at it. Same count as [D-137](#) asks for, a different operation. Several orphans from one purge are listed with a per-row choice and *apply to all*, per [D-126](#) and [D-100](#). | `agreed` | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md), [70 Migration](70-migration.md) | closes [OQ-037](91-open-questions.md); records the specialise move that had only been drawn |

| D-157 | 2026-08-22 | **An unsatisfiable model is caught where it is created, reported rather than blocked — but data entry against it is barred.** An attribute with multiplicity `1` or `1..*` whose permitted set is **empty** demands an answer nobody can give; [D-056](#) deliberately gave it no control state because it is a broken model, not a state. **The check runs at the moment of narrowing** — setting an allow-list to nothing, or deleting the last member of a branch — where [D-050](#) is already asking whether there are consequences and [D-126](#) already names them. Catching it at data-entry time would be far too late: a different person, weeks later, in front of a form they cannot fill in. **Not blocked**, because an author may legitimately be mid-rebuild and about to refill the branch — it is reported as a model conflict ([D-054](#)) and shown in the preview ([D-101](#)). **But data entry for that model stays barred until it is resolved**, since there the conflict is not a message but a dead end. | `agreed` | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md), [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md) | closes [OQ-053](91-open-questions.md) |

| D-158 | 2026-08-22 | **A model author may replace a validator message, and it lives as a label — per validator.** A shipped message is a software string with named placeholders; an author-written one becomes **content** and needs a locale like any label. So it is a **label**, not a free text field: same table, same resolution walk, same fallback chain, with the shipped text underneath so the chain never ends empty. **Placeholders survive** — an author message may contain `{min}` and `{max}`, and the **named-placeholder format stays mandatory**, since a sentence assembled from fragments cannot be translated into a language that orders them differently. **The owner's correction: it is per validator, not per attribute** — an attribute may carry range, format and uniqueness checks, three messages rather than one. So **`labels` gains a `path` column**, exactly as `record_values` did ([D-134](#)) and as override addressing already uses ([D-045](#)): `owner_id` = the edge, `path` = the validator, `role` = message. Third place, same mechanism, no new concept. **And catalogue-versus-case is not a choice** — it is the two ends of one walk ([D-015](#)): set on the validator node it applies everywhere, set at a use site it overrides there. ⚠️ **What the author may not change is the offered correction** — that is behaviour, and behaviour is code ([D-036](#)). The author says **what went wrong**, not **what the system should do about it**. | `agreed` | [40 I18n](40-i18n.md), [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md) | closes [OQ-030](91-open-questions.md) |

| D-159 | 2026-08-22 | **The descent has two inputs, both loaded before it starts, and there is only one mode.** The owner: *is it necessary for the renderer to work properly? Yes — because depending on the data, the renderer may have to adapt its output.* That settles it: the record is not decoration passed along for display, it is an **input the renderer reasons about**, so it must be there in full when the renderer is called. Loading model subgraph and record tree up front follows from **CD-7** — a descent that fetches per edge is N+1 by construction. **Where they disagree, the model decides what is drawn and the renderer changes nothing.** A record written against an older model version ([D-060](#)) keeps values for edges the model has since lost — those are simply not drawn, and **not touched**: they stay in `record_values` the way a parked record stays ([D-123](#)), because removing them is a migration ([D-061](#)) and migrating is not the job of drawing. A new edge without a value is not an error, it is the empty control state. The divergence is **reported as a record conflict** ([D-054](#)) in the list, not thrown at the reader mid-page. **The renderer never writes, not even to tidy up.** | `agreed` | [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md) | closes [OQ-061](91-open-questions.md); raises [OQ-070](91-open-questions.md) |
| D-160 | 2026-08-22 | **The preview renders a test data pack, not an empty shell.** The question had been whether *no record* is really *the default record*, which would make the preview an ordinary rendering rather than a special case. The owner took the better route: **the preview loads the test data and renders that** — *so I have data available too*. It is the same single mode, but fed with realistic values instead of defaults, which is what makes a preview worth looking at: a form of empty fields shows that the structure exists, a filled one shows whether it **reads**. It also means the test data packs earn a second job beyond testing, so they stay maintained. **Defaults remain the fallback** where no pack covers the model. ⚠️ The **model editor** is not covered by this and does not need to be: it walks the same edges, it just pulls a different renderer out of the registry. One descent, two renderer sets — not two descents. | `agreed` | [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md) | with [D-159](#) |

| D-161 | 2026-08-22 | **The relation kind is not asked, it is read off the target's branch.** The owner: *if I point at a node that can only be an aggregation, then I should only be able to pick aggregation.* So the author never answers *composition or aggregation?* at all — they pick a **target**, and the kind follows: a target under `Model` yields **aggregation**, a target under `Kompositionen` yields **composition**. The `kind` enum ([D-036](#)) stays in the data, because reading an edge must not require walking to the target's branch, but it becomes a **derived** value, never an input. This kills the error class [D-133](#) exists to prevent — a supplier accidentally composed into an order, so every order breeds its own supplier and the catalogue dissolves into copies — not by validating it afterwards but by never offering it. It also removes the need for the switch this question asked about: a per-node *not compositable* flag would be a second truth beside the branch, and the first one to contradict it. ⚠️ **Gap found 2026-08-23, when the owner asked whether this was really safe:** the rule named `Model` and `Compositions` and said nothing about `Primitives`, where the data types live — closed by [D-193](#). **And the owner asked that it be checked again against real cases during testing**, recorded rather than waved away: it is a rule that decides storage, and being wrong about it is expensive. | `agreed` | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md) | closes [OQ-068](91-open-questions.md) |

| D-162 | 2026-08-22 | **Moving a node between the branches rewrites every edge that points at it, and the data comes along.** There is no per-use-site exception: *leaving it a composition over here* would make the node both, and the branch would stop being the truth ([D-161](#)). Where both are genuinely needed, that is **two nodes** — the catalogue node under `Model` and a node under `Kompositionen` inheriting from it, the specialise-instead-of-flip pattern of [D-156](#). ⚠️ **A first proposal — that the two directions are asymmetric and one of them loses the shared identity — was wrong, and the owner corrected it:** by [D-133](#) the kinds differ in **ownership**, not in layout, so the records are the same records either way and simply move with the node. **`Kompositionen` → `Model` always works**: the part stops being owned and becomes independent. *Its one exception:* multiplicity 1 is stored **inside** the owning record by path, so those values must be lifted into records of their own — mechanical and lossless, and since multiplicity sits on the edge, the same node can need lifting on one side and not on the other. **`Model` → `Kompositionen` works exactly when each affected record has a single user** — the owner's condition, and it follows from what composition *is*: the part dies with the whole, so a record used five times cannot be owned by one of them. Otherwise it is a case for the conflict resolver ([D-054](#)), whose resolutions are **a copy per using record** (corrected from *per use site* by [D-214](#)) or **stay an aggregation**. That keeps the copying an author's named decision rather than a side effect of moving. Afterwards the condition is no longer a check but a guarantee: a composition **is** sole ownership. Recorded in the changelog like any model change ([D-061](#)). | `agreed` | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md), [70 Migration](70-migration.md) | with [D-161](#) |

| D-163 | 2026-08-22 | **At multiplicity 1 the author no longer chooses inline versus own record — the branch chooses for them, and they must be told.** [D-133](#) makes the storage follow from kind and multiplicity, and [D-161](#) then makes the *kind* follow from the branch the target sits in. Put together, picking a target under `Model` turns even a *have exactly one of* into **its own record**, where inline would have been the natural shape. The owner, on being shown this: *then it would be a record of its own in the model — whether that is sensible I rather doubt. But yes, the user has to be pointed at it, and at the effects.* So the modeller **says so at the moment the target is picked**, and says what changes: the value becomes an independent thing that can be found, referenced from elsewhere and deleted on its own, rather than a property that lives and dies inside its owner. **It is not forbidden** — sometimes exactly right, an address used once today and reused tomorrow — but it must never be a surprise discovered later, in a search result nobody expected. This is the same *don't ask what cannot matter, do say what will* stance as [D-050](#), one step further: **do not ask a question the branch has already answered, but do report the answer.** | `agreed` | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md), [50 Persistence](50-wordpress-persistence.md) | refines [D-133](#), [D-161](#) |

| D-164 | 2026-08-22 | **Records do not share the model's identity space. Model objects keep theirs.** The owner: *is there any reason at all why they should carry the same ids? I don't see one.* ⚠️ **My argument for one space did not survive being checked** — I claimed a value reference is ambiguous because it may resolve to a node or to a record, but [D-131](#) already says **the target type's placement decides which**, per edge, deterministically. Nothing is ambiguous, so nothing is bought. **And one space costs something real:** MySQL has no sequences, so a shared space means a hand-built allocator — a single row behind a lock, sitting on the hottest write path in the system — where per-table `AUTO_INCREMENT` is free and proven. **Nodes and relations stay in one shared space**, because there the ambiguity is genuine: a setting hangs on a node or on an edge and the model does not say in advance which, so `owner_id` must be a real foreign key over both ([D-090](#)). **The one place a column may hold either sort is the changelog**, and it stores what it refers to alongside — frozen history rather than a duplicated fact ([D-065](#)). Separate spaces also keep the two layers honestly apart: model tables in the hundreds, data tables in the millions, and no invitation to merge them, which is the wall the previous project ran into. | `agreed` | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md), [50 Persistence](50-wordpress-persistence.md) | closes [OQ-036](91-open-questions.md) |

| D-165 | 2026-08-23 | **Correctness has no limit, speed is promised to about three conditions, and the reporting case gets a flat projection rather than a cleverer query.** The generic query accepts **any number** of conditions and always answers correctly — a hard cap would have to be explained in an error message, and it would always sit in the wrong place. What is *promised* is narrower: one condition is an indexed range scan on `(edge_id, value)`, two or three are an intersection of such scans, and past that it is a chain of joins no index makes free. Saying so is an honest description of an attribute-value store ([P11](50-wordpress-persistence.md)), not a weakness waiting to be optimised away. **The stage above** — the owner's own way of thinking about growth, from the colour-code round — is **one flat projection per model**: a table with a column per attribute, filled from the records. Ten joins become an ordinary `WHERE`, and the normal case pays nothing for it. ⚠️ **The projection is a cache and never a place where anything is stored** — never written to, only rebuilt, exactly the standing of a materialised computed value ([D-072](#)). Without that line it would be the next duplicated fact. **Full-text search stays outside this**: a separate mechanism ([OQ-064](91-open-questions.md)), not a harder version of the same one. | `agreed` | [50 Persistence](50-wordpress-persistence.md) | closes [OQ-056](91-open-questions.md) |

| D-166 | 2026-08-23 | **What cannot have been meant is removed by the converter; what might have been meant is questioned by the validator.** Raised by the owner: *on input we should avoid needless errors — leading and trailing spaces first of all; and one could offer an option that interior spaces are not allowed, only hyphens. That would be a property of the text field, or part of the validator.* The two halves land in different places, and the dividing line is whether intent is ambiguous. **Leading and trailing whitespace is stripped silently by the converter** ([V8](00-vision-and-scope.md)) — nobody has ever meant a trailing space, so there is nothing to ask, and a dialogue about one would be an imposition. **Interior spacing is a validator**, because `BC 547 B` is a perfectly ordinary way to write it: an attribute-level setting like `min`, overridable along the resolution chain ([D-015](#)), which **offers** the corrected form rather than enforcing it — exactly the correcting validator [V9](00-vision-and-scope.md) has called for since the first dictation. ⚠️ *The converter/validator split is my call*; the owner named both candidates and left the choice open. **Two consequences that must not be forgotten:** trimming runs **before** duplicate detection and before the uniqueness check ([D-154](#)), or `Bauteil` and `Bauteil ` become two records nobody can tell apart. And the hyphen rule is **not** about findability — the search column normalises spacing and punctuation away anyway ([OQ-064](91-open-questions.md)), so `BC 547 B` and `BC-547-B` already find each other. It is about tidiness and duplicates, which is what makes *offer* rather than *enforce* defensible. | `agreed` (confirmed by the owner 2026-08-23) | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md), [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md) | — |

| D-167 | 2026-08-23 | **Contains is the default in the quick search, an operator field carries the filter, and there is no wildcard character.** The search structure is a **normalised column per record**, written on save from the fields that are shown ([D-112](#)) — lowercased, spacing and punctuation stripped, so `bc547b` finds `BC 547 B`. ⚠️ **One normalisation function, shared with duplicate detection**: two rules that *almost* agree are the kind of fault nobody finds until a user swears the part is not there and it is. **The quick search is contains, without syntax** — the owner: *the user mostly wants to type some fragment, and that is usually contains*. Making them type a `*` to get what they meant would be a fee charged in syntax to save milliseconds, and honestly counted they are milliseconds: one `LIKE '%x%'` over a single narrow indexed column at tens of thousands of rows. ⚠️ *This reverses half of my own earlier proposal*, which had made contains an on-request fallback. **Kept from it:** a short delay after the last keystroke, and **prefix hits ranked first** — which also answers *why is the thing I typed exactly not at the top*, and lets the indexed hits appear while the rest catches up. **The filter is the other surface and gets a visible operator field** — is · contains · begins with · greater · between — because there the user is deliberately building a condition, and `greater than` has no wildcard equivalent at all. **No `*`:** a third syntax beside those two that can do nothing they cannot, needed only for anchoring, which nobody wants to express — and typed by accident it searches literally for a star and reports nothing found, with no hint why. **The growth stage — n-gram full text or a token table — stays deferred by the owner** until real data exists to look at: *I don't think we will have millions. Other projects maybe. Let us look at the data first.* Like every index here it is derived and rebuildable ([D-016](#)). | `agreed` | [50 Persistence](50-wordpress-persistence.md), [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md) | closes [OQ-064](91-open-questions.md) |

| D-168 | 2026-08-23 | ⚠️ **Überholt durch [D-217](#)** — was hier steht, gilt **nicht mehr unverändert**; die geltende Fassung steht dort. *Welcher Teil fiel, sagt die Zeile weiter unten.* **Purpose is part of the render context, and searching is the third one. The registry is keyed by type *and* purpose.** Raised by the owner: *shouldn't there be a renderer context for searching — so that the text renderer, handed a search option, renders the field differently, with a selection for contains or begins-with?* Two purposes are already distinguished in all but name — **display** and **edit**, which the renderer options carry as *editable / not*. **Search** simply becomes the third, and the operator field then falls out in the right place instead of being built beside it. **Why this rather than a separate search widget:** which operators make sense depends on the **type** — text has *contains* and *begins with*, a number has *greater* and *between*, a date has *before* and *after* — and that knowledge already sits with the type's renderer and converter. A separate search surface would be a second place that knows what a number is, and the two would drift. **Three consequences.** (1) A renderer under the search purpose returns **not a value but a condition** — operator plus operand — which feeds the query builder of [D-165](#) directly; the filter's operator field is therefore not a component of its own but *what a text renderer looks like when its purpose is search*. (2) The offered operators are **declared at the type**, once, beside the converter — never a switch on type name, which the rules forbid outright. (3) It hands us a free home for **not searchable**: a backward-read computed value ([D-140](#)) is calculated at read time and stands in no index, so it simply has no search renderer and never appears in the filter — no special case, no greyed-out option, no error message. **The price:** registry lookup takes type **and** purpose, and needs a **fallback** — with no search renderer registered, the edit renderer plus the type's default operators — or every type would need three renderers written before anything worked at all. ⚠️ **Die Registry-Schlüssel-Hälfte ist ersetzt** durch [D-217](#); der Rest gilt. | `agreed` | [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md) | with [D-167](#) |

| D-169 | 2026-08-23 | ⚠️ **`ACTIVE`, bestätigt am 2026-09-01, und die Ergänzung räumt einen Denkfehler von mir aus.** *Ich hatte einen Widerspruch zwischen dieser Zeile und dem Ziel «Portierbarkeit» gemeldet. Es ist keiner, sondern eine Arbeitsteilung — der Eigentümer: «der Kern ist WordPress-unabhängig. Und aber wir wollen dann im Rand die Fähigkeiten von WordPress nutzen, mit zusätzlichen eigenen Entwicklungen, wenn WordPress das nicht bietet, was wir brauchen … die beiden arbeiten zusammen.»* **Portierbarkeit entsteht durch die Grenze, nicht durch Verzicht:** der Kern bleibt frei, damit der Rand voll zugreifen darf, ohne etwas nachzubauen. **Yes, a WordPress plugin — and WordPress is used to the full, not held at arm's length. What is borrowed gets written down.** The owner: *I want to use the plugin in WordPress and use every capability that helps us. But we should keep track of the WordPress specifics so that moving the whole thing to another framework is easier later.* So this is not a portability-first design: nothing is reimplemented to stay neutral, and no capability is passed over because another framework might lack it. Portability is served by **knowing what was borrowed**, not by borrowing less. ⚠️ **The mechanism for recording it is not yet decided** — the owner suggested a `wp` prefix or suffix on the relevant code; see [OQ-071](91-open-questions.md). **And [CD-1](../../CLAUDE.md) becomes testable rather than aspirational:** the core's tests run **without a WordPress bootstrap**, the boundary's tests with one. Two separate runs. Any WordPress call that drifts into the core then fails on the first run instead of being discovered years later by whoever tries to reuse it. | `agreed` (`ACTIVE`, vom Eigentümer bestätigt 2026-09-01) | [00 Vision and scope](00-vision-and-scope.md), [50 Persistence](50-wordpress-persistence.md) | closes [OQ-009](91-open-questions.md); opens [OQ-071](91-open-questions.md) |

| D-170 | 2026-08-23 | ⚠️ **`ACTIVE`, bestätigt am 2026-09-01 — mit einer offenen Aufgabe.** *Das hier beschlossene Hauptbuch der geliehenen Fähigkeiten, «eine Zeile je geliehener Fähigkeit und was sie ersetzen müsste», **existiert nicht**: es steht als Kästchen in einem Diagramm in [50](50-wordpress-persistence.md) und in der Prosa, aber es gibt keine Liste. Beschlossen und nicht gebaut — und durch die Bestätigung von [D-169](#) wird es wichtiger, nicht unwichtiger: je mehr der Rand ausnutzt, desto mehr ist aufzuschreiben.* **The namespace is the marker, a ledger catches what the namespace cannot, and a second boundary is the intended shape of a port.** No `wp` prefix on class names — [CD-1](../../CLAUDE.md)'s split is a namespace split, so everything under the boundary namespace is WordPress-facing by definition and a prefix would only repeat the path ([CD-9](../../CLAUDE.md)). Prefixes stay where WordPress itself demands them: tables, hooks, options. **Beside it a short ledger, kept as the code grows** — one line per borrowed capability and what would have to replace it — because a namespace catches WordPress *calls* but not WordPress *assumptions*: the capability model, the block editor's data shapes, `dbDelta`'s idea of a schema, the shape of an admin screen. Those are what a port would actually founder on and no folder contains them. **The owner's reason for the namespace is the strategy itself:** *later you could leave the WordPress namespace in place and add another one, so the same code serves different frameworks — or different versions.* Two additions make that pay off, ⚠️ *both my call*. (1) **The core states its own needs as interfaces it owns** — storage, translation, clock, id allocation — which the boundary *fulfils*. A core that is only ever called into offers a second boundary nothing to plug into. (2) **The boundary translates, it does not decide.** The plan holds only while the boundary stays thin; if domain logic lived there, a second boundary would duplicate it rather than replace plumbing, and the two would drift apart. In practice: a boundary class branching on something the domain means is in the wrong place. | `agreed` (`ACTIVE`, bestätigt 2026-09-01; das Hauptbuch fehlt) | [50 Persistence](50-wordpress-persistence.md), [00 Vision and scope](00-vision-and-scope.md) | closes [OQ-071](91-open-questions.md) |

| D-171 | 2026-08-23 | **WordPress is not underneath the core, it is around it, and every arrow points inward.** Recorded because the owner pictured it as a classic layer stack — *the bottom layer binds WordPress, everything above is generic* — and that picture inverts the dependency: with WordPress at the bottom the core calls **down** into it, which is exactly what [CD-1](../../CLAUDE.md) forbids and what makes the core impossible to lift out. In the boundary picture WordPress touches the core at two places and **both point the same way**: from outside in — hooks, REST routes, admin screens, blocks, CLI *use* the core; and from inside out — the core declares what it needs (storage, translation, clock, id allocation) and WordPress *fulfils* it, the interface belonging to the **core**. The owner's intuition was right and standing on its head: there is a layering, but the specific layer is not *below*, it is *outside*, and the generic middle knows nothing of it. That is what allows a second boundary to be set beside the first ([D-170](#)) without the core noticing. The German term used in the documents is **Anschlussschicht**. | `agreed` | [00 Vision and scope](00-vision-and-scope.md) | clarifies [D-009](#) |
| D-172 | 2026-08-23 | **Undo is in scope. It is a step forward, not a rewind, and its reach is the trash.** The owner asked what mistakes a person actually wants to take back: a wrong name — *he can fix that himself, but it is nice if a button does it* — and the serious one, *changing an attribute and then noticing: oops, I have broken my data*. **The revert is recorded as a new change**, never as a rewind: history is extended, never rewritten, or the changelog stops being the migration script ([D-061](#)). **Version numbers order events; shape decides compatibility.** This answers the owner's own puzzle — revert to the earlier model and suddenly version 1 data fits again, version 2 data does not, version 3 fits. Saying *it is version 1 again* would make one number mean two moments and a record stamped `1` ambiguous. Instead the revert is a **new** version that happens to have the **same shape** as the old one, and a record is in conflict when the model differs **in the parts that record uses** — not by distance between numbers. ⚠️ **So nothing new is needed**, which was the owner's actual question: the existing tools suffice provided compatibility is computed from shape. **The reach is nameable in one sentence a user can be told: as long as the trash has not been emptied.** [D-123](#) parks instead of deleting and [D-159](#) leaves orphaned values untouched; together they *are* the window in which taking something back is honest. Once purged it is gone, and the interface must not pretend otherwise. | `agreed` | [70 Migration](70-migration.md), [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md) | closes [OQ-057](91-open-questions.md) |
| D-173 | 2026-08-23 | **Migration is not an undo case, and importing existing WordPress tables is its own tool.** The owner: *migration I would not see as an undo case — that is something I actively do.* Undo answers *I did not mean that*; migration answers *I mean this now*. Two different intentions, and merging them would give both the wrong safeguards. **Separately, a real need was named:** the owner has many WordPress standard tables of the same kind that want moving into the plugin's own tables, and *since other users probably have the same problem, one could provide a tool for it*. That makes the importer a **boundary tool** ([D-171](#)) — deliberately not core, because reading `posts`, `postmeta` and `terms` is WordPress knowledge through and through. It gets its own concept document when the domain core is locked, not before; how it is told what maps to what is [OQ-072](91-open-questions.md). | `agreed` | [70 Migration](70-migration.md) | opens [OQ-072](91-open-questions.md) |

| D-174 | 2026-08-23 | **A seeded node carries two marks: *came from the seed* and *changed since*.** The concrete case is the plugin update. A new version ships an additional role or relation kind ([D-151](#)) and it has to slot in; but if the owner has meanwhile **renamed or adjusted** a seeded node, the update must not paint over it. Without a mark the updater can tell neither what it is responsible for nor whether that thing has been touched. ⚠️ **One mark is not enough** — *came from the seed* says the updater **may** feel responsible, not whether it still **should**. So: **untouched → updated silently; changed → left alone and reported as a model conflict** ([D-054](#)), for the owner to decide. This keeps the two failure modes apart that a single flag would merge: an update that destroys the owner's work, and an installation that silently stops receiving improvements. The alternative — treat seeded nodes as ordinary ones and let updates touch nothing — was rejected: it makes every future addition a manual chore and guarantees installations drift apart. Note this is not the template flag of [D-122](#); that one protects, this one attributes. | `agreed` | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md), [70 Migration](70-migration.md) | closes [OQ-065](91-open-questions.md) |

| D-175 | 2026-08-23 | **Content packs: named sets of model content that can be installed and removed again. The seed is simply the pack that ships.** Raised by the owner, who has more of these in mind than the product had accounted for — *recipes, PC hardware tests from CPU through memory to expansion cards, ESP projects* — and wants to *offer the user the chance to load them in, and to delete them out again once he has looked enough*. **This unifies four things the concept had lying about separately:** the seeded nodes, the test data packs the preview renders ([D-160](#)), the standard tree harvested from the legacy project, and these. All of them are the same object. The provenance marks of [D-174](#) therefore gain one field rather than a new mechanism: not *from the seed* but **from pack X**, still paired with *changed since*. **Removal is already solved by machinery that exists.** A pack lifts out cleanly exactly when **nothing outside points into it** and **nothing inside was changed**; otherwise the conflict resolver ([D-054](#)) lists what stands in the way and removal runs through the trash like any other deletion ([D-123](#)). The happy case is the common one, and it follows from the owner's own phrasing — *once he has looked enough*: whoever only looked built nothing on top, so the try-it-and-remove-it path is the clean one by construction. The messy case arises only where someone took the pack seriously, and there they **should** be asked. ⚠️ **A hard line, my call: a pack is data, never code.** Nodes, relations, settings, labels and example records yes; its own renderers, validators or converters no — a pack that needs behaviour **declares a dependency** on it instead of carrying it. Otherwise *install a pack* eventually means *run someone else's code*, which is a wholly different question from *let me look at the recipes*. | `agreed` (confirmed by the owner 2026-08-23) | [00 Vision and scope](00-vision-and-scope.md), [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md) | generalises [D-174](#), [D-160](#) |

| D-176 | 2026-08-23 | **The pair is *Modell* and *Daten*, single unit *Datensatz*. *Bestand* is rejected.** ⚠️ *I proposed `Bestand` as a collective for everything entered after the model, on the grounds that `Daten` strictly covers the model too. The owner was not convinced it was needed, and he is right — that is worrying about a problem nobody has.* A pack ([D-175](#)) therefore contains **a model, and optionally some data**, which reads perfectly well without a new word. Recorded because a term invented once tends to come back, and the reason for dropping it should outlive the conversation. | `agreed` | [01 Glossary](01-glossary.md) | — |
| D-177 | 2026-08-23 | **A pack declares what it requires and marks what it contributes. It may add to another pack's branch and never change another pack's nodes.** The owner saw two possible shapes — extract the shared base into its own pack and depend on it, or let a pack check whether the data is already there and supplement it — and was unsure which was better. **They are two halves of one answer**, and his own observation resolves it: *even when the main node is shared, the sub-nodes differ*. Because the provenance mark of [D-174](#) sits **on the individual node** rather than on the pack as a lump, a pack can deliver **into** an existing branch without it becoming unclear who owns what. So: **required** content is factored out and declared as a dependency; **contributed** content goes into the existing branch carrying its own mark. Removal stays answerable because every node knows where it came from. ⚠️ **One rule holds it together: add yes, alter never.** Without it two packs come to disagree about one node and no update can decide who is right. | `agreed` | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md) | extends [D-175](#) |
| D-178 | 2026-08-23 | **Pack authorship is a recording mode, not a label per node.** The owner asked how one declares that what is being made now belongs to a pack. Tagging each node individually is the obvious answer and nobody sustains it. Instead the tree is put into *I am working on pack XYZ* and everything created while it runs carries the mark. ⚠️ **Because modes lie, it has to be conspicuous while it is on** — a mode that looks like the normal state produces exactly the mistake it was meant to prevent. It is **off by default and out of the way**, since almost nobody ever authors a pack. A correction for the ordinary case comes with it: assigning an existing branch to a pack afterwards. | `agreed` | [20 Interaction](20-interaction.md) | extends [D-175](#) |
| D-179 | 2026-08-23 | **The tree keeps everything the legacy tree did — the crowding is answered by a menu, not by removing abilities.** The owner on the old tree: *everything in it turned out to be really good*, with one reservation, that the row is **a bit overloaded** with seven controls. So the row keeps what is used constantly and a **context menu holds everything**, reached by right-click **and by a `⋯` on the row** — touch has no right-click, and without it the menu would be unreachable on half the devices this runs on. The same action in both places is a second route, not a second truth. **Up and down stay** despite dragging: moving one position by drag is fiddly and a button is exact — *reorder by one* and *move elsewhere* are different intents. ⚠️ **And the header's `+` and the row's `+` mean different things** — expand-all versus add-a-child. The owner felt those icons wanted replacing; the reason is not that they are ugly but that one wears the other's sign. **What cannot apply is absent rather than greyed** ([D-050](#)) — visible already in the legacy tree, where the last child has no *down* arrow, and it is what makes a seven-control row readable at all. | `agreed` | [20 Interaction](20-interaction.md) | — |
| D-180 | 2026-08-23 | **Deleting a node asks whether the branch goes too; dragging moves whole branches, several at once; duplicating lands directly beneath with an indexed name.** **Delete** offers two operations and guesses neither: the whole branch, or this node alone with *the children hung on the father*. ⚠️ **The second is less harmless than it looks** — the tree is inheritance ([D-041](#)), so children reattached to the grandparent **lose what they inherited from the deleted node**. That is precisely the move of [D-155](#) with orphaned overrides promoted per [D-156](#): the same rules reached from a different button, and no new machinery. **Drag and drop** takes the node's entire branch with it, and a multi-selection takes all of theirs — *rather practical*, in the owner's words, and it is what the tree is for: restructuring is the daily work of modelling, and doing it one node at a time is what makes people avoid doing it at all. Moves run through [D-155](#) and, where the target branch changes the relation kind, through [D-162](#). **Duplicate** places the copy **immediately under its original** with a trailing index — not at the end of the list — because the owner's working rhythm is to duplicate several times and rename afterwards, and that only works if each copy lands where the eye already is. | `agreed` | [20 Interaction](20-interaction.md) | — |

| D-181 | 2026-08-23 | **What may be picked is a property of the use site, not of the node. The legacy *hide* control is retired.** The owner on the eye icon: it hid a node, *is currently not really used anywhere*, and was aimed at keeping some nodes from being **selectable** — because in a hierarchy the person choosing should land on a **leaf**, not on an intermediate node. ⚠️ **It went unused because it sat on the wrong object.** The same node is a legitimate choice in one place and a mistake in another: when **modelling**, picking the branch is exactly right — *I want something from this branch* is how a type is named ([D-041](#)); when **entering data**, a leaf is wanted. A flag on the node would have to be both at once. A setting **at the use site** says it without strain and inherits along the chain ([D-015](#)), so *leaves only* is set once high up rather than on every attribute. The owner reached the same place himself — *maybe that is really an option of the renderer* — and it arrives as a value in the render context ([D-168](#)). **Default is leaves only.** He could think of no case for choosing an intermediate node, and that is a finding rather than a gap: the exception must justify itself the first time it turns up. If a pure view-tidying need ever appears, it comes back as a **filter on the view**, never as a stored flag on the node. | `agreed`, default reversed by [D-238](#) | [20 Interaction](20-interaction.md), [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md) | retires the legacy hide control |
| D-182 | 2026-08-23 | **Branch deletion stops being its own button and becomes the second answer in the delete dialog.** The rightmost hierarchy icon in the legacy tree deleted the whole branch, beside a separate delete for the node. [D-180](#) already replaced the guess with a question — *branch, or this node with the children hung on the father* — and that question now covers both controls. **One red button in the row instead of two**, and with it goes the chance of hitting the wrong one: two destructive controls of the same colour, side by side, differing only in glyph, is a trap laid for a tired person. | `agreed` | [20 Interaction](20-interaction.md) | refines [D-180](#) |

| D-183 | 2026-08-23 | ⚠️ **Die Speicherhälfte ist überholt durch [D-522](#)** — *ob ein Knoten Datensätze trägt, entscheidet seither, ob er Felder hat. **Was bleibt, ist `holdsData()` als Antwort auf «hat er Exemplare»**, woran die Vorschau hängt.* **Having data is read off the branch: `Model` and `Kompositionen` have data, everything else is means to an end.** The owner, returning to the previous day's discussion: *everything under Model has data, everything under Definition has none — it is only a means to an end.* Same construction as [D-161](#): the branch says it, not a switch on the node, which is why it stays consistent without anyone maintaining it. `Kompositionen` is included because a part has data, it merely has no independent existence ([D-135](#)). ⚠️ **What the non-data branch is called is open** — the owner asked for a better word than `Definition`; see [OQ-073](91-open-questions.md). | `agreed` | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md) | opens [OQ-073](91-open-questions.md) |
| D-184 | 2026-08-23 | **Small lists do not get a second storage mechanism; they get a cheaper editor.** The owner's worry: *simple things with one or two columns — enum values — do I really want to bloat my model with those?* ⚠️ **The bloat is an interface problem wearing a model problem's clothes.** Five enum values **are** five nodes, and should be: referenceable, translatable, renameable, and extensible by a sixth without touching code. It only *feels* heavy when creating them costs five dialogues. So the answer is **a compact list editor**, never a parallel place to keep values — the moment there are two kinds of value in the system, every code path has to handle both, which is exactly the trade this project lost once already. | `agreed` | [20 Interaction](20-interaction.md), [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md) | — |

| D-185 | 2026-08-23 | ⚠️ **Überholt durch [D-187](#) und [D-188](#)** — was hier steht, gilt **nicht mehr unverändert**; die geltende Fassung steht dort. *Welcher Teil fiel, sagt die Zeile weiter unten.* **The branch without data is called `Bausteine`.** Chosen over `Definition` (a model node is a definition too, so the word does not separate anything), `Vokabular` (precise but abstract at first sight) and `Werkzeug` (suggests behaviour, and these are not behaviour). What lives there — data types, constants, aggregates, complex types — is **what models are built out of**, and the word has to tell a new user without an explanatory sentence why nothing they type will ever be kept there. ⚠️ **Der Zweigname ist ersetzt** durch [D-187](#) und [D-188](#); der Rest gilt. | `agreed` | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md), [01 Glossary](01-glossary.md) | closes [OQ-073](91-open-questions.md) |

| D-186 | 2026-08-23 | **V1–V9 are confirmed, and V5 gains a nuance that changes what it forbids.** The foundation had been carrying *not yet confirmed — do not implement from this file* while 185 decisions cited it; the owner ratified it on 2026-08-23 and [00 Vision and scope](00-vision-and-scope.md) moves to `agreed`. **Transcription readings settled:** *Taximodeller* → **Taxonomy Modeller**; *über die Gastronomie auf* → **taxonomy**, so it was the same word mangled twice rather than the noise I had dropped it as; *Hutknoten* → **root node**. **The one that matters:** *Grundsätzlich sind alle Knoten gleich* stands, but the owner added *there are specialisations — that statement was about the **data model** and **how nodes are stored***. So V5 forbids a second **shape in the database**, not specialisation itself: one kind of node, stored one way, with specialisation arriving through configuration ([V7](00-vision-and-scope.md)). ⚠️ *Worth keeping, because V5 as first written could have been read as banning specialisation altogether, and several decisions lean on it.* | `agreed` | [00 Vision and scope](00-vision-and-scope.md) | ratifies V1–V9 |
| D-187 | 2026-08-23 | **English is the standard for every term. German exists as a translation, where one is needed.** The owner: *basically I would like all terms in English. Even when I use the German ones, because we are talking in German — always use the English terms. That is the standard, and we provide a German translation where needed, only where needed.* ⚠️ **This supersedes the German branch name of [D-185](#)** and the mixed set it produced — `Model` English beside `Kompositionen` and `Bausteine` German. It is not cosmetic: the base name is the locale-neutral last resort ([D-022](#)) and the thing that appears in every path, every changelog entry and every error message, while what a person *sees* is the label in their locale ([D-020](#)). So the base names go English and the German lives where German belongs, in the labels. **Conversation stays German; the vocabulary does not.** | `agreed` | [01 Glossary](01-glossary.md) | supersedes the naming half of [D-185](#) |

| D-188 | 2026-08-23 | **The three branches are `Model`, `Compositions` and `Primitives`.** Applying [D-187](#) to the names [D-185](#) had left half German. `Primitives` was chosen over `Building Blocks` — in a Gutenberg project *blocks* is already taken and *the blocks branch* would be ambiguous in every conversation — and over `Vocabulary`, which reads well but is one step more abstract than the things it holds. German labels carry `Bausteine` where a German reader needs one, which is exactly the division of labour [D-020](#) exists for: English base name, translated label. | `agreed` | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md), [01 Glossary](01-glossary.md) | supersedes the name in [D-185](#) |
| D-189 | 2026-08-23 | **Counts counts what there are several of, and the branch says what that is.** Under `Model` and `Compositions` there are **records**, so it counts records. Under `Primitives` there are none, so it counts **children** — which for an enum is precisely the interesting number, how many values are in it. The owner put it in one line: *where there is data the data is counted, and where there is none the sub-nodes are.* One question — *how many of these are there* — answered differently because different things exist in different branches. No switch, no setting, and no case distinction on node names, which the rules forbid outright. | `agreed` | [20 Interaction](20-interaction.md) | follows [D-183](#) |

| D-190 | 2026-08-23 | **The detail view's order is the order of dealing with a node, and it is not a special screen.** Top to bottom: **what acts** (buttons), **what cannot be changed** (a band of chips), the **name** — *because that is what you change first* — then **display**, then the **attributes**, then the **preview**, and last the **relations**, collapsed. The owner walked this order out loud and it is not taste: it is the sequence in which a person actually works on a node, and writing it down keeps a rebuild from reshuffling it for looks. ⚠️ **And the screen is not special-cased:** it is a series of attributes rendered under the **edit** purpose ([D-168](#)) inside a frame. Otherwise there are two ways to draw a field — the official one and the one the admin screen was built with — and they drift. | `agreed` | [20 Interaction](20-interaction.md) | — |
| D-191 | 2026-08-23 | **Attributes show their core and load their detail on demand — for two reasons that agree.** A row carries name, type, multiplicity, kind, default, read-only, hidden and inherited; everything else sits behind a per-attribute expander, and **relations sit collapsed at the bottom and are not even queried until opened**. The owner named both halves: it *keeps things clear*, **and** *you do not have to load all the information, only what is shown, and only for the attribute you are editing*. ⚠️ **Recorded because legibility and loading rarely point the same way**, and when only one argument is remembered the pattern gets discarded at the next rebuild as mere styling. **Density is likewise a requirement, not polish** — the owner: *it is all still relatively large; it would be good to fit more on one screen while staying clear*. Two rules that follow and cost nothing: fields are sized **to their content, not to the container** — a ten-character name does not need a field spanning the window — and fixed facts are **chips, not rows**, which the legacy meta band already does and which is the densest part of that screen. | `agreed` | [20 Interaction](20-interaction.md) | — |
| D-192 | 2026-08-23 | **Three sections the legacy detail screen has no place for, because they were decided after it was built.** **Conflicts** go **above the name**: [D-054](#) reports rather than blocks, but a report nobody sees is a block with extra steps, and an unresolved conflict on this node is the most actionable thing on the screen. **Provenance** joins the fixed band as chips — which pack a node came from and whether it has been changed since ([D-174](#), [D-175](#)) — because that pair decides whether the next update will touch it. **History** goes to the bottom beside relations, collapsed: every change with before and after ([D-061](#)), and the place where taking something back lives ([D-172](#)); the legacy *last modified by* is one line of it. A **parked** node ([D-123](#)) must announce itself too, but as a state of the whole screen rather than a section within it. | `agreed` | [20 Interaction](20-interaction.md) | — |

| D-193 | 2026-08-23 | **Inside `Primitives` the sub-branch decides: data types yield a composition, constants yield an aggregation. Closes a gap in [D-161](#).** The owner, checking the earlier rule: *a node decides aggregation or composition — and if it is a data type it is always a composition.* Right, but not for everything in that branch. **An `int` is a composition**: the value lives in the record, and at multiplicity 1 literally inside it ([D-133](#)). **A `Basiseinheit` is also a primitive and is an aggregation**: it points at a **node**, at `Gramm`, which is modelling-time content ([D-131](#)). So the branch still decides — **one level deeper**. ⚠️ *This distinction is not an invention: the legacy tree already separated `Data Types` and `Complex Datatypes` from `Konstanten`. I flattened it when I gathered everything under one name in [D-185](#), and lost it in the process.* The principle survives untouched — *the place says it, not a switch* — it only needed the third and fourth row of the table. **To be re-verified against real cases during testing**, at the owner's request. | `agreed` | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md), [01 Glossary](01-glossary.md) | closes a gap in [D-161](#) |

| D-194 | 2026-08-23 | **`Is template` is retired: provenance says what it said. Protection is separated out and narrowed.** The owner: *`Is template` meant it is part of the base tree — our scaffold, the thing we ship. It used to be a field on the node; that is now somewhat replaced by the pack.* Attribution is therefore covered by [D-174](#) and the toggle can go. ⚠️ **But it cannot take the other half with it.** [D-122](#) hung **deletion protection** on that same mark, and *came from the shipped pack* is far too wide for that — a sample recipe ships from us too and must be deletable. So the two are split: **provenance is information**, **framework is protection**, and the shipping pack sets the framework mark on the few nodes the machinery itself stands on — those the bindings point at ([D-120](#)). The refusal still says *this is a framework type* rather than *a binding points at this*, which was [D-122](#)'s whole point. **Neither is a toggle in the detail view any more**; provenance shows as chips in the fixed band ([D-192](#)) and framework is not a user's decision at all. | `agreed` | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md), [20 Interaction](20-interaction.md) | supersedes the flag of [D-122](#) |
| D-195 | 2026-08-23 | **`slug` is not ours.** The owner: *slug was something WordPress set; if we do not have it, it can be left out.* It is therefore a boundary concern ([D-171](#)) — where a URL needs one it is a setting held at the boundary, and it has no business among the four fixed attributes ([D-082](#)). | `agreed` | [50 Persistence](50-wordpress-persistence.md) | — |
| D-196 | 2026-08-23 | **The seeded label roles are `form`, `table`, `select`, `symbol` and `help`, offered together on the node.** Read off the legacy detail screen and confirmed by the owner: those were the standard fields, *and being able to fill them right there with the node is quite helpful — we should keep that*. ⚠️ *`long`, which the glossary carried, was my invention; the owner's `help` is the same thing under a better name.* Roles remain nodes and remain extensible ([D-151](#)) — this is the seeded set, not the possible set. Filling them beside the name matters because a label written later is a label never written. | `agreed` | [40 I18n](40-i18n.md), [01 Glossary](01-glossary.md) | — |

| D-197 | 2026-08-23 | **There is one chooser in the product. It picks and it creates, and it always looks the same.** The owner states it as a design rule for the whole interface, not a preference for one screen — and notes he had to explain it at length to the previous assistant, which is why it now sits at the **top** of [20 Interaction](20-interaction.md) rather than inside a section. It may take options for different scenarios; it may not become a second widget. ⚠️ **It corrects my own split of an hour earlier**, which had given aggregation *a list of references* and composition *a blueprint for parts*. The owner: *I don't know whether you are separating aggregation and composition too much. If I have an aggregation whose target does not exist yet, I have to be able to enter it. With composition I always have to — but the case exists for aggregation too.* So the mechanism is one, and the kinds differ only in **which path is the usual one**: creating for composition, picking for aggregation. On pre-entering values for a composition he was honest about the limit of his own certainty — *for data fields definitely; for a real composition in the form of a row I am not sure at all* — and settled it on the cheaper side: *we lose nothing by making it just as enterable*. **Two rules survive from the corrected draft:** defaults are chosen with the **same chooser as data entry**, never a special one for defaults; and **nothing is edited inside the field** — a chip is the reference renderer ([D-105](#)), label plus link, and the link takes you there. **Inline selection is the exception**: in many places it makes no sense, and where a choice is genuinely hard a proper dialogue that helps is better. | `agreed` | [20 Interaction](20-interaction.md) | — |
| D-198 | 2026-08-23 | **A control's state follows from what is actually choosable.** The owner's example: a select holding **one** entry is greyed out — nothing is being chosen — and becomes live only when more than one option exists.
