This is [D-050](#) — *do not ask what cannot matter* — moved from dialogues down to individual controls, and the owner asks for it to be **checked everywhere and kept consistent** rather than decided screen by screen. A control that poses a question with exactly one possible answer is not helping; it is asking a person to prove they read it. | `agreed` | [20 Interaction](20-interaction.md) | — |

| D-199 | 2026-08-23 | **The relations section holds one direction and is renamed `Used by`.** Working through it showed that half the legacy section was already on the page: outgoing non-inheritance edges **are** the attributes table ([D-031](#)), the parent edge is a chip in the fixed band, the children are the tree. The owner: *everything going out of the current node is in the attributes. As long as that stays so, we do not need to show them in the relations.* What is left is the **incoming** direction, which appears nowhere else. **`Used by`** — *Verwendet von* — over `Incoming`, which names the arrow rather than the meaning, and over `Referenced by`, since *reference* already carries three jobs in this concept. ⚠️ **The owner's condition is recorded with it:** the section may hold one direction only because every outgoing edge is currently visible elsewhere; if an outgoing edge ever appears that is neither an attribute nor inheritance, the section has to grow back or it quietly stops being complete. **It is an impact estimate rather than a listing** — the same list the conflict resolver would pull, but beforehand and voluntarily: who breaks on delete ([D-122](#)), where a move flips the kind ([D-162](#)), what points into a pack from outside ([D-177](#)), who inherits a renamed label ([D-015](#)). ⚠️ **Model and data stay apart here:** on a node it lists the **attributes of other nodes typed by this node**; which *records* point at a *record* is a different question belonging to the record's own screen. | `agreed` | [20 Interaction](20-interaction.md) | — |

| D-200 | 2026-08-23 | **The four remaining questions are deferred deliberately, each with the event that reopens it.** None can be answered today without inventing, and [PR-4](../../CLAUDE.md) forbids inventing to fill a gap — but a deferral with a named trigger belongs in the log rather than in a list of things nobody decided. **[OQ-069](91-open-questions.md) · Views** — reopens *the first time a figure appears that belongs to no node*. Turnover per supplier per month is not a property of a supplier and not one of an order; the owner's own question, *where would I put it*, then has no answer. Until then a view would be a second place where calculations live. **[OQ-070](91-open-questions.md) · Renderer resolution in a long list** — reopens *the first time a table is slow*. The question is whether the renderer is per column and only its output per row, and it cannot be settled against a table that does not exist. What is written down is why it is delicate at all: since [D-159](#) a renderer may adapt to the value, so it is *usually* constant down a column and *not reliably* constant. **[OQ-072](91-open-questions.md) · What the importer is told** — reopens *when the domain core is locked*. Before that an answer has no ground: the trap is whether the importer **creates** a model from a table or only **fills** one that exists, and guessing a model from a table reproduces the relational shape the product exists to leave behind ([V1](00-vision-and-scope.md)). **[OQ-074](91-open-questions.md) · An enum filled at runtime** — reopens by the owner's own criterion, *as soon as we work with the project* and notice it missing. A first plausible answer is already recorded and keeps the boundary intact: not *data may create model*, but *the model declares in advance where it may be extended*. ⚠️ **Deferred is not decided.** Each of these still has to be answered before the part of the system it belongs to is built; what is decided is only that answering it **now** would be guessing. | `agreed` | [91 Open questions](91-open-questions.md) | defers [OQ-069](91-open-questions.md), [OQ-070](91-open-questions.md), [OQ-072](91-open-questions.md), [OQ-074](91-open-questions.md) |

| D-201 | 2026-08-23 | **A *view* and a *report* are different things, and only one of them was ever the deferred question.** The owner asked for the English word **`View`** to be used — *it does come close to the database view, even though we do not think in databases* ([D-187](#)) — and then, in describing what he might want, described something else entirely: *one could produce reports. Exporting a parts list, say, or creating an invoice.* **A view is a named computation belonging to no node**; that stays deferred on its existing criterion ([D-200](#)), and the interim stands: aggregated figures hang on nodes as computed fields ([D-140](#)). **A report is prepared output.** Nothing is computed that was not already there — it is **rendered**, in a form that leaves the building. That puts it on the renderer side, not with calculation, and it means the two must not share a deferral, a mechanism or a word. ⚠️ **A guess, marked as one:** a report may need no new mechanism at all — a descent over a subtree under a further **purpose** beside display, edit and search ([D-168](#)). Worth testing when the stage arrives; not claimed now. **Reports are a later stage** by the owner's own placement. | `agreed` | [60 Calculation](60-calculation.md), [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md) | sharpens [OQ-069](91-open-questions.md) |

| D-202 | 2026-08-23 | **Correction to [D-201](#): a report does compute, at output time, and it joins. Both halves of my characterisation were wrong.** The owner: *a report can contain calculations, like the front end does, but ones that arise at the time of the export — at the time the output is produced. And a report can contain raw data, or accumulate it, or combine it with other data. Linked, joined, however you want to put it.* ⚠️ *I had written that nothing is computed in a report that was not already there, and guessed it might be no more than a descent over a subtree under a further purpose. The first is simply untrue, and the second cannot survive the second half: **a join across unrelated records is not a descent**.* What stands from [D-201](#) is the separation itself — a **view** is a named calculation belonging to no node, a **report** is prepared output — and that they must not share a word. What falls is the claim that a report needs no new mechanism. **Rules for a report are stored** — *this is how the report looks* — which makes a report a configured thing rather than a written one, consistent with everything else here. | `agreed` | [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md), [60 Calculation](60-calculation.md) | corrects [D-201](#) |
| D-203 | 2026-08-23 | **Views, reports and the list-rendering optimisation are Release 2.** The owner placed all three there in one sitting, which gives the roadmap its first real content and the concept its first *not now, and here is where instead*. **Views and reports** — *from my side we could start thinking about the view, I just do not know whether that stretches the scope a bit. I would say: pencil view and report in for release two.* **The renderer-per-row optimisation** ([OQ-070](91-open-questions.md)) — *is this optimisation needed for stage one? With the small tables we have now, a BOM with at most twenty or thirty rows, I do not think it weighs at all in terms of speed.* ⚠️ **He is right and the correction is mine to make:** thirty rows by ten columns is three hundred **registry lookups**, and a registry lookup is a hash lookup in memory, not a query. It is not measurable. The optimisation earns its place at thousands of rows. **One requirement survives the deferral and is recorded now because it is hard to retrofit:** the owner — *if errors occur then that is a conceptual error on our side and it has to be visible so we can react*. A precomputed row template meeting a value that needs a different renderer must **fail loudly, never draw quietly wrong**. That holds before the optimisation exists. | `agreed` | [95 Roadmap](95-roadmap.md) | closes [OQ-070](91-open-questions.md); places [OQ-069](91-open-questions.md) |

| D-204 | 2026-08-23 | **Runtime extension: declared per branch including by whom, usable at once, reviewed afterwards. Closes [OQ-074](91-open-questions.md).** Three roles differ not in ability but in trust — the **modeller** building the tree in admin, the **editor** in Gutenberg who needs a value right now, and the **visitor** entering data in the front end, whose use case the owner calls unclear and wants kept open rather than opened. **Who may extend belongs in the declaration itself:** the model already states *where* it may be extended ([OQ-074](91-open-questions.md)'s first answer), so the same declaration states *by whom* — one more field, no new concept, inherited along the chain ([D-015](#)). **Use immediately, judge later.** If a value were unusable until approved, the block this feature exists to remove would simply move: the owner's own starting point was *I have no wish to go back into the design view just to make that one entry*, and waiting for a person is worse than going yourself. **Nothing new has to be built for the review.** The addition carries a provenance mark — *made during data entry, not yet reviewed* — which is [D-174](#) with one more value; the list of pending additions has the shape of the conflict resolver ([D-054](#)), reporting rather than blocking; and a **no** is an ordinary deletion, two-stage ([D-123](#)), with whatever used the value becoming a conflict the resolver already handles. **Afterwards it is an ordinary node.** Distinguishable while pending, indistinguishable once approved — a permanent second class of node would have to be known to every code path, and how it came about is the changelog's job ([D-061](#)), which is where provenance belongs once it is history. | `agreed` | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md), [20 Interaction](20-interaction.md) | closes [OQ-074](91-open-questions.md) |
| D-205 | 2026-08-23 | **A pending review shows in the tree, and it propagates up through collapsed ancestors.** The owner asked for it to be visible in the tree and left the form to me. **The propagation is the part that matters:** a marker only on the node itself is useless, because the node is usually inside a collapsed branch and nobody expands a whole tree on the off-chance. So a collapsed parent carries the marker of anything pending beneath it — **filled means this node, outline means something below it** — and following it downwards is how you find the node without searching. It is **not red**: red is deletion and conflict here, and a pending review is neither wrong nor destructive, merely unfinished. It is also distinct from the `Counts` badge ([D-189](#)), which answers a different question. | `agreed` | [20 Interaction](20-interaction.md) | with [D-204](#) |

| D-206 | 2026-08-23 | **The front end is composed of small blocks, one node each, and the reference does the joining.** The owner: *I would rather not build one huge block that queries all the data for several connected nodes. I would enter the board — it has the parts list as an attribute, but only an id is set there. Further down the page, at a suitable place, I would display the parts list.* **This is [D-105](#) arriving in the front end and needs nothing new:** a reference is drawn as label plus link and **does not descend**, so a board block *cannot* pull its parts list in even if someone wanted it to. Where the list appears — further down the same page, or on another page entirely — is the page builder's free choice, and in both cases it is **a second block**, never a larger first one. The link needs no dynamism: it is a label and an address, so as the owner put it, *many things can be done after saving*. | `agreed` | [20 Interaction](20-interaction.md) | — |
| D-207 | 2026-08-23 | **A comparison block resolves to the nearest common ancestor, and that is the tree doing its job.** The owner compares mainboards — P4 against P4, but also 286 against 386 against 486 — and describes it himself: *a comparison of similar data types. Similar, because there may be differences in detail; then I would fall back on the parent node, compare only what they have in common, and show the rest separately.* ⚠️ **That is not a workaround.** A node's type **is** its inheritance line ([D-041](#)), so the nearest ancestor covering every subject **is** the set of shared attributes — the block never has to guess what is comparable. Walk up until all subjects lie beneath one node, compare the attributes found there side by side, and show what each subject additionally carries beneath its own column. **Confirmed by the owner 2026-08-23, with a refinement:** what is not comparable is **moved below** the comparison rather than mixed into it — *and perhaps it can be hidden too, and only shown when the user wants to see it*. That ordering is the point of the block: the shared attributes are what a comparison is **for**, and letting the specialities interleave would bury exactly the rows someone came to read. Hiding them behind a disclosure keeps them available without letting them compete. | `agreed` | [20 Interaction](20-interaction.md) | — |
| D-208 | 2026-08-23 | **A list block is a node plus a restriction.** *A list of manufacturers — in a larger sense persons, or organisations rather — I would like to show as a list. So I choose a node, and restrict the data I want to display.* Two inputs, both already modelled: **which node** supplies the records, and **which of their attributes** are shown. No new concept — the restriction is a choice of attributes and each is drawn by the ordinary renderer under the display purpose ([D-168](#)). | `agreed` | [20 Interaction](20-interaction.md) | — |

| D-209 | 2026-08-23 | **`long` is gone. It was renamed `help`, and the fallback chain ends there.** Resolves contradiction 1 of [_harvest/contradictions.md](_harvest/contradictions.md): [D-196](#) removed `long` from the seeded roles and called it my invention, while [D-151](#) hangs the fallback chain on `long` and calls the chain broken without it. The owner: *`long` is gone, it is defined as `help`.* So the chain is `<role>` → **`help`** → `node.name`, and everything [D-151](#) and [D-153](#) say about `long` now says `help`. ⚠️ *The gap was mine: I retired an anchor without checking what hung from it.* | `agreed` | [40 I18n](40-i18n.md), [01 Glossary](01-glossary.md) | supersedes `long` in [D-020](#), [D-151](#), [D-153](#) |
| D-210 | 2026-08-23 | **A record keeps its version stamp. Resolving touches only what actually conflicted.** Resolves contradiction 2: M14 and [D-060](#) said that once everything is resolved only records of the **current** version remain, while [D-172](#) says compatibility is decided by **shape**, so a record stamped `v1` can sit happily against `v4`. The owner: *the stamp only exists where there are conflicts; otherwise the underlying version stays the same.* So there is **no mass re-stamping** — a migration walks the records a change actually broke and leaves the rest alone, and records at several versions are a normal steady state rather than an unfinished job. The stamp therefore means **written against**, not *checked against*. ⚠️ **Supersedes M14's closing sentence and the same claim in [D-060](#).** It also removes work nobody would have noticed being wasted: rewriting a version column across every record of a model to record that nothing happened to them. | `agreed` | [70 Migration](70-migration.md) | supersedes part of [D-060](#) |
| D-211 | 2026-08-23 | ⚠️ **Überholt durch [D-229](#)** — was hier steht, gilt **nicht mehr unverändert**; die geltende Fassung steht dort. *Welcher Teil fiel, sagt die Zeile weiter unten.* **A medium has two locations on purpose, carries its provenance, and lives in the WordPress media library.** The owner, from the legacy project: a media type that *can be a URL, or stored as a file, or both — I download the current state but point at the URL*. ⚠️ **Both is not indecision, it is two different questions answered:** the **copy** is there when the source disappears, the **URL** is there when a newer version exists. Same shape as [D-143](#) — one side tracks, the other freezes. **Provenance travels with it:** an image carries its source attribution and that attribution is **displayed with it**, which makes a medium a small composite — location, copy, source — rather than a scalar. At multiplicity 1 that is a composition and lives **inside** the record ([D-133](#)); no new mechanism. **Storage is the WordPress media library** — the owner: *we should use the strengths of WordPress there* ([D-169](#)) — which brings upload, permissions, thumbnails and sizes already built. Our model holds the identifier, the URL and the attribution. ⚠️ **The consequence he named himself:** a file can be deleted in WordPress without our deletion machinery ([D-123](#)) knowing, because the file is not ours. His answer: *I should notice whether the file is there or not — and if we do not get told, then I check before display whether it is still there and, if need be, show only the link.* So **existence is checked at display time**, and a missing file degrades to its link rather than to an error. ⚠️ **Die Speicher-Hälfte ist ersetzt** durch [D-229](#); der Rest gilt. | `agreed` | [50 Persistence](50-wordpress-persistence.md), [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md) | — |
| D-212 | 2026-08-23 | **A project fact sheet is not a new block — it is [D-206](#) under the display purpose.** The owner floated *a project profile that outlines what is needed*, and worked out mid-sentence that it is *a table block of one record*, which [D-206](#) and [D-168](#) already produce between them. Recorded because a block that was considered and found unnecessary is worth more than one that was added: it is evidence the block set is holding rather than growing per use case. | `agreed` | [20 Interaction](20-interaction.md) | — |

| D-213 | 2026-08-23 | **An update corrects what the author never touched. [D-119](#) is precised, not overturned.** Resolves contradiction 3: [D-119](#) said updates *never overwrite*, [D-174](#) that an unchanged seeded node is *updated silently*. Made concrete: we ship a seeded role misspelt `Symobl` and fix it in the next version — does the typo go away for someone who never touched it? **Yes.** [D-119](#)'s protection was always meant as *never overwrite **an author's edit***, and that is how it now reads. The cost is named rather than hidden: the same rule applies when we change a shipped label **deliberately** because we think the new wording better, and then something does change under the user unasked. That is accepted — the protection belongs to what **they** touched, not to everything we ever delivered, and the *changed since* mark ([D-174](#)) is what tells the two apart. ⚠️ *The owner's confirmation came through a garbled dictation line — "aktuelle Lesierung verbessern auch" — read as agreement with the recommendation. Worth a second look if it ever behaves surprisingly.* | `agreed` | [70 Migration](70-migration.md) | precises [D-119](#) |
| D-214 | 2026-08-23 | **Only one composing edge may point at a node — and my objection to that rule rested on my own misuse of a term.** Resolves contradiction 4. I had argued that [D-137](#)'s model-level check was too strict, since `Bestellung.Adresse` and `Kunde.Adresse` are two edges yet each individual address still has one owner. ⚠️ **The owner rejected the example itself:** *the address is a design error. An address is obviously an aggregation. And if I want a second edge to the same address, it cannot be a composition any more.* A part that **can** belong to two different wholes is not a part; that every instance happens to have one owner hides the modelling mistake rather than curing it. Where something genuinely is needed in two wholes, it is an aggregation, or two specialised nodes ([D-156](#)). **And the contradiction dissolves once the right word is used.** [D-162](#) offered *a copy per use site* — but a **use site is a relation** in this concept, and what was meant is *per using record*: five orders reach one address through **one** edge, and the copy is made per order. So both checks stand and address different levels — **one edge at model level, one owner per record at data level** — and the resolution operates on the data level, where several users can exist. [D-162](#)'s wording is corrected accordingly. | `agreed` | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md), [70 Migration](70-migration.md) | upholds [D-137](#); corrects wording in [D-162](#) |

| D-215 | 2026-08-23 | **The term is `Data Pack`, not `Pack`.** The owner, confirming [D-175](#): *exactly — data pack makes it more precise.* And it does the work the longer name costs: the hard line in [D-175](#) is that a pack is **data and never code**, and a bare *pack* invites the reader to assume otherwise, since in every other plugin ecosystem a package is something you install and run. The name now carries the rule. | `agreed` | [01 Glossary](01-glossary.md) | names [D-175](#) |

| D-216 | 2026-08-23 | **The `number` column holds a plural **category**, not a switch. [D-153](#) loses its `provisional`.** [D-153](#) put singular and plural on the label row as a **column** rather than as a `plural` role — correctly, because the roles carry genuinely different texts and *the plural of `table`* would have been the plural of nothing. The owner marked it `provisional` because he was not sure the matter was settled. ⚠️ **What the doubt was pointing at only became visible today:** two numbers are enough for German and English and for almost nothing else. Polish has three forms, Arabic six. A boolean column makes those languages unrepresentable, and the shortfall would surface at the first translation, long after the schema is fixed. So the column holds one of the **Unicode plural categories** — `one`, `other`, and where a language needs them `zero`, `two`, `few`, `many`. German simply uses two of them. It costs nothing now and stops the first Slavic translation from being a schema problem. ⚠️ *Recorded as an example worth remembering: the owner's unease was right while his stated reason was not the whole of it — the flag was worth keeping until someone found what it was flagging.* | `agreed` | [40 I18n](40-i18n.md) | resolves the doubt in [D-153](#) |

| D-217 | 2026-08-23 | ⚠️ **Überholt durch [D-236](#)** — was hier steht, gilt **nicht mehr unverändert**; die geltende Fassung steht dort. *Welcher Teil fiel, sagt die Zeile weiter unten.* **One node, one renderer. The purpose is passed to it, not keyed on. Supersedes the registry-key half of [D-168](#) and resolves contradiction 1.** ⚠️ *I had put purpose — display / edit / search — into the **key** of the registry. That implies a node holds several renderers at once, one per purpose, and the owner ruled it out: **a node cannot have several renderers at the same time**.* What survives from [D-168](#) is the valuable half — purpose belongs in the **render context**, and **search is a full third purpose** beside display and edit, which the owner asked not to be forgotten. **The contract already carries the mechanism:** `supports()` is where a renderer declares which purposes it can serve. So *not searchable* stops being a special case — a backward aggregate ([D-140](#)) simply has a renderer that does not support `search`, and the attribute never appears in the filter. A missing capability, not a missing registration. **The registry has two jobs**, the second named by the owner as *a connection to the possible users*: at render time, *give me the renderer of this name*; at configuration time, **which renderers are eligible for this node at all**. **Choosing one is the chain we already have** ([D-015](#), [D-084](#)): one stands on the node, and the **using attribute may override it** — *I give the whole thing a new look by using it*. Where several are eligible, one is marked **default per type** and applies until someone chooses. ⚠️ **A last-resort renderer is allowed but is a fault indicator, not a floor:** a node without a renderer is an error and must look like one. Covering it with a quiet grey default is how such a fault survives three weeks unnoticed. ⚠️ **Die Ein-Renderer-Hälfte ist ersetzt** durch [D-236](#); der Rest gilt. | `agreed` | [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md) | supersedes part of [D-168](#) |
| D-218 | 2026-08-23 | **Read-only renders under the display purpose — and a read-only field with neither calculation nor default is a model conflict.** Asked whether a read-only attribute inside an edit form gets the edit renderer with its input suppressed, the owner was unambiguous: **no input at all** — *I fixed that as the tree builder, and then there is no input there.* So the renderer is asked for **display**, and [D-095](#) stands unchanged: read-only removes the **input**, not the **field**. ⚠️ **And the owner added a check nobody had asked for:** a read-only attribute with **no calculation** and **no default value** can never hold anything. It is a dead field that looks like part of the form. It is therefore reported as a model conflict ([D-054](#)) at the moment it is configured ([D-050](#)), with the two repairs named: **set a default**, or **remove the read-only**. This is the validator-offers-a-correction pattern of [V9](00-vision-and-scope.md) applied to the model itself rather than to data. | `agreed` | [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md), [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md) | — |

| D-219 | 2026-08-23 | **A representation is a converter plus a renderer, and the mapping is model data. The only axis is invertible or not.** ⚠️ *I had proposed a split between **notation** — the same information written differently, like a resistor's colour rings — and **encoding**, a user-defined mapping like a traffic light. The owner destroyed it in one sentence: **a traffic light with ten colours is a resistor colour code.** The line was a count of members, not a property of the thing, and no concept can stand on that.* **One construct instead:** the **converter** is the mapping (value → rings, value → `2k7`, value → `104`, value → green), the **renderer** is the form (text, one colour, a sequence of colours, stars). Both already exist ([V8](00-vision-and-scope.md)). ⚠️ **Several converters may be *eligible* for a type, but exactly one is *in effect* per rendering** — the same shape [D-217](#) gives renderers. That is how [V8](00-vision-and-scope.md)'s *one converter* and [D-077](#)'s *may carry several* fit together: the first is the effect side, the second the stock side, and which one applies is a setting with a default and a per-use-site override. The owner put the effect side plainly: *we currently allow only one converter, not several.* **The surviving distinction is one the concept already had:** invertible converters can be **written into** ([D-076](#)) — colour rings, `2k7`, `104`, a coil code — and non-invertible ones are output only — traffic light, stars, maturity levels. **What this buys:** resistor, coil, capacitor, shorthand notations, traffic lights and star ratings all become instances of **one** mechanism; standards ship as data packs ([D-175](#)); users build their own the same way. **Code is needed only where the mapping cannot be written as a table or a rule** — that is the boundary the owner was looking for, and it does not run between traffic light and colour code but between *writable down* and *must be computed*. **A consequence found while walking a resistor through:** a converter attaches **at the level whose value it encodes** — `2k7` encodes number and prefix, so it hangs on `quantity` and works at once for capacitances, lengths and baking recipes; the colour rings also encode tolerance, so they hang one level up. | `agreed` | [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md), [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md) | generalises [D-149](#), [D-150](#) |
| D-220 | 2026-08-23 | **Whether something is one field or several is decided by the model, not by the display. The composed type is the unit of rendering.** The owner: *I type `2k7` and it lands in two different fields, the `2.7` and the `k` — I think we need some kind of combinatorial renderer here.* ⚠️ **No new kind of renderer is needed**, because those are not two fields: they are **members of one value** that is incomplete without them. [D-150](#) already made `Widerstandswert` a composed type so that the colour code could stay an ordinary attribute renderer; the prefix is the same move one level down. Members stay individually reachable because a composition at multiplicity 1 lives **inside** the record, addressed by path ([D-133](#), [D-134](#)) — so *tolerance ≤ 5 %* is still searchable without tolerance being an attribute of its own. **Two stages, and the first needs no code:** a **generic composite renderer** draws any composed type — one control per member, each drawn by the member's own renderer — so a new composed type works immediately, for dimensions, addresses or baking recipes alike. On top of it, a **shorthand** control accepting `2k7` is a converter ([D-219](#)) and exists only where a shorthand exists. ⚠️ **This does not mean two converters at once:** one renderer with one converter serves **both directions** — drawing the form and parsing what is typed into it ([D-149](#)). Showing a value in two different forms **at the same time** is two renderings of one attribute, not one rendering with two converters. Both controls stand side by side and write into the same value — [D-149](#)'s two coupled controls, no longer a special case for resistors but the general build. **Choosing is two settings that narrow each other:** the renderer decides the **form** and declares which result shape it can draw; the eligible converters are those producing that shape. Pick the renderer and the converter list narrows itself — and if one remains it is used without being asked, by [D-198](#). | `agreed` | [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md), [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md) | — |
| D-221 | 2026-08-23 | **There is no *fixed value*. There is a restriction that collapses to one.** Walking a resistor through the configuration turned up that the unit must be pinned to `Ohm`, or every author picks it again on every part — and the legacy settings screen had a **fixed value** on composite members for exactly that. The owner saw both designs and was undecided: state the fixed value directly, or **forbid everything else** and let it follow. ⚠️ **The restriction wins, because it is not a second construct.** Say *only `Ohm` is permitted* and that **is** the fixed value — and the interface follows for free, since [D-198](#) already makes a control with one possibility disappear. A separate *fixed* setting beside the permitted set would be the classic duplicated fact: *fixed = Ohm* against *permitted = {Ohm, Volt}*, with nothing to say which wins. **Restrictions narrow downwards and never widen.** A use site further down may restrict further; it may not reopen, or *only Ohm* guaranteed nothing in the first place. Whoever genuinely needs another unit does not need a `Widerstandswert`. | `agreed` | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md), [20 Interaction](20-interaction.md) | — |

| D-222 | 2026-08-23 | **A decision is the record of a choice, not a wall. Revising one is normal work.** The owner, in his own words: *sometimes things change when you think further, and then you have to be able to revise old decisions. That was exactly the problem in the old project — old decisions were taken as **fact**, and when a new problem turned up that fact prevented loosening them or building a better concept.* Recorded as a decision rather than left in the rule file, because it is the one thing that decides whether this log helps or paralyses. **The log exists to make revision cheap and traceable, not to make it impossible.** A superseded row is not an embarrassment: it keeps the reasoning that was true at the time, and names what replaced it. What is forbidden is the *silent* revision — changing a position without saying which row it overturns, which is how a concept comes to hold two answers at once. ⚠️ This is what [PR-3](../../CLAUDE.md) and the architecture-rule discipline in [CLAUDE.md](../../CLAUDE.md) are **for**; they were written as bookkeeping and they are really an escape hatch. | `agreed` | [90 Decision log](90-decision-log.md) | — |
| D-223 | 2026-08-23 | **Automatic is a default, never a fact.** ⚠️ *I had said that where only one converter qualifies it is taken "without being asked" — misapplying [D-198](#). The owner caught it: he may want to say **no colour code here, it bothers me**, and under my wording he had no way to.* The two questions are different, and only one of them is dead: **which** converter supplies a colour sequence may genuinely have one answer, so that control disappears; **whether** this use site wants a colour form at all always has two, so that control stays. **The general rule:** anything the system chooses on the user's behalf must be revocable at the use site — [D-032](#)'s two-fold principle applied to automatic choices. It follows that **an automatic choice must be visible**: a field showing red-blue-green with nothing anywhere saying *presentation: colour code* and no place to change it is the kind of magic nobody can switch off later because nobody can find where it came from. Concretely, the shorthand control accepting `2k7` appears **automatically where an invertible text converter exists, and can be switched off** — and refusing a colour form needs nothing special, since choosing the ordinary composite renderer at that use site already is the refusal. | `agreed` | [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md), [20 Interaction](20-interaction.md) | corrects my reading of [D-198](#) |
| D-224 | 2026-08-23 | ⚠️ **Verschoben in den [Dachboden](92-veraltete-entscheidungen.md) am 2026-08-29** — überholt durch [D-236](#), das den Dekorator als **Konfigurationsbegriff** durch die Renderer-Liste ersetzt. *Es trug bis dahin **keinen** Vermerk und las sich als gültig. Was weiterlebt, sagt D-236 selbst: ein Dekorator bleibt ein **Implementierungsmuster**, aber nichts, was ein Benutzer konfiguriert.* | `superseded` | — | ersetzt durch [D-236](#) |
| D-225 | 2026-08-23 | ⚠️ **Überholt durch [D-226](#)** — was hier steht, gilt **nicht mehr unverändert**; die geltende Fassung steht dort. *Welcher Teil fiel, sagt die Zeile weiter unten.* **Invertible replaces, non-invertible decorates — and that settles when a second occurrence is needed.** Whether an alternative form **replaces** the value's presentation or sits **beside** it does not need a setting: it follows from the axis already in [D-219](#). An **invertible** converter loses nothing — the colour rings *are* the number, `2k7` *is* 2.7 kΩ — so it can stand alone and replaces. A **non-invertible** one hides the value if shown alone: you see green and no longer know the figure, so it **decorates** beside it. Default, and overridable — on a summary sheet someone may well want the traffic light alone. ⚠️ **This splits what looked like one gap into two answers.** *Number and colour rings side by side* are two invertible forms, which replace each other, so that genuinely needs **two occurrences of the attribute** — the chain gaining a last link, `installation → root → ancestors → node → use site → occurrence in a block`, with nothing moving into the model: the model still knows only that a resistor has a value. *Number with a traffic light* needs no second occurrence at all, because that is decoration ([D-224](#)). Two different needs, two different answers, and neither has to be bent to cover the other. | **superseded by [D-226](#)** | [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md), [20 Interaction](20-interaction.md) | — |

| D-226 | 2026-08-23 | **Invertibility decides only whether a form can be written into. Replace or decorate is a free choice. Supersedes [D-225](#).** ⚠️ *[D-225](#) hung two questions on one axis: invertible converters **replace** the value's presentation, non-invertible ones **decorate** beside it. The owner took the axis apart: **whether it is invertible or not is not what matters to me right now. The difference is only that I can enter a colour code and get a resistance and a tolerance out of it, and from red-amber-green I cannot get back to the value.*** So invertibility answers exactly one question — **may a person type in this form** ([D-076](#)) — and nothing else. **Layout is an independent, explicit choice at the use site:** a form may stand in place of the value or beside it, whichever the author wants, for invertible and non-invertible forms alike. What remains of [D-225](#) is a **default and only a default**: a form that would hide the value if shown alone starts out decorating, because you would otherwise see green and no longer know the figure. **The consequence is a simplification.** Number **and** colour rings together no longer require the attribute to appear twice in a block — *value, plus colour rings* is one setting in one place, and it works in the detail view too, where there is no column list at all. Listing an attribute twice in a block stays **possible** and useful for two genuine columns, but it is no longer the only route. ⚠️ *This is the first live use of [D-222](#) — a decision revised the same day, visibly, because thinking further showed the rule answered **when it is sensible** while the owner was asking **when it should be possible**.* | `agreed` | [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md), [20 Interaction](20-interaction.md) | supersedes [D-225](#) |

| D-227 | 2026-08-23 | **The rule counts possibilities, not entries. Refines [D-198](#) and resolves its clash with [D-056](#).** [D-056](#) said a single entry under an **optional** multiplicity must **not** be greyed out; [D-198](#) said a select holding one entry **is** greyed out. Both stand — [D-198](#) was simply too short. At `0..1` there are **two** possibilities, the one entry or **nothing**, so the control is alive; at `1` there is genuinely one and it disappears. ⚠️ Same shape as the correction in [D-223](#): *which converter* may have one answer while *whether to use that form at all* always has two. **The test is never *how many rows are in the list* but *how many outcomes can this control produce*.** | `agreed` | [20 Interaction](20-interaction.md) | refines [D-198](#); resolves its clash with [D-056](#) |

| D-228 | 2026-08-23 | **[P5](50-wordpress-persistence.md) stands untouched; a projection is not storage, and there are few of them by design.** Resolves the first contradiction of the persistence pass: [D-066](#) forbids *a table per model* — the sentence that makes run-time modelling possible at all — while [D-165](#) proposed exactly that as a flat projection for the reporting case. ⚠️ *Calling it a cache answered the duplicated-fact objection but not the real one: a table per model means `CREATE TABLE` and `ALTER TABLE` at run time, triggered by a user adding an attribute.* **The rule is kept by narrowing what it governs: [P5](50-wordpress-persistence.md) binds everything that *holds* data, and a projection holds nothing.** What makes run-time DDL dangerous is data loss and migrations that depend on user data; a cache table has neither — it may be dropped and rebuilt at any moment and never counts toward the plugin's schema version. **Three conditions make that honest:** the system runs with **no** projection at all — missing one makes a query slower and still correct; where the host forbids DDL there simply are none, with no error and no half state; and `dbDelta` never knows about them. ⚠️ **And the owner supplied the constraint that matters more than the DDL argument:** *I have nothing against a table per schema in principle — but we are so fine-grained that we could very quickly have a great many tables. That is why we said at the outset to go the generic way.* So projections are **opt-in and few**: switched on for the one model whose reporting is slow, never created automatically per model. A cache built for everything **is** a schema; one built for three models is an index. | `agreed` | [50 Persistence](50-wordpress-persistence.md) | keeps [D-066](#), narrows [D-165](#) |

| D-229 | 2026-08-23 | **A medium is an ordinary type under `Model`, not a special construct — and it knows its own MIME type.** Resolves the second contradiction of the persistence pass, that a WordPress attachment id fits neither identity space ([D-164](#)) nor any typed value column. ⚠️ *I had proposed making the medium a composed type with the attachment id as an inline text member, and dismissed a dedicated store as too much. The owner asked what the dismissed option would buy, and the answer turned out to be decisive: **[D-211](#)'s source attribution is a fact about the file, not about the part.*** With the inline shape, one photo used by fifty parts writes the photographer's name into fifty records, and correcting it means correcting fifty — the duplicated fact the rules forbid outright. Three more things follow: *which records use this file* becomes a scan; the same file cannot be shared; width, height, checksum and licence have no home. ⚠️ **And the dedicated store needs no new table.** `Medium` is a **type under `Model`** with attributes attachment id, URL, source, licence — an ordinary record in `records` and `record_values`, aggregated by whoever uses it ([D-161](#)). *What uses this image* becomes the ordinary **Used by** ([D-199](#)); deletion is the ordinary trash ([D-123](#)); [D-211](#)'s *is the file still there* check lives on the medium record, in one place. The attachment id survives as a **text** attribute — an opaque key of a foreign system, so the core sees text and knows nothing of WordPress ([D-171](#)). The only cost is comfort: attaching an image now creates a record, and the user must not notice. **The owner extended it: the medium detects its own MIME type** — image, source code, document. Detected at the boundary, since WordPress already knows it, and **stored**, or every display would have to touch the file. **The renderer dispatches on the MIME family to a registered presentation**, so [D-217](#) holds — one node, one renderer — and supporting a new kind of file means **registering one more**, never extending a switch. ⚠️ *This is also the first concrete instance of what [OQ-070](91-open-questions.md) feared: in a list of media the renderer genuinely differs from row to row.* | `agreed` | [50 Persistence](50-wordpress-persistence.md), [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md), [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md) | supersedes the storage half of [D-211](#) |

| D-230 | 2026-08-23 | **A medium is drawn along two axes: kind is detected, degree is configured — one renderer, not their product.** The owner: *sometimes in a table you do not want the image rendered at all — a preview only, or just a link, or a schematic file-type symbol with a link. And elsewhere I really do want the whole image, or the PDF embedded.* **Kind** comes from the detected MIME type ([D-229](#)) — image, source code, document. **Degree of presence** is a setting at the use site: symbol · link · thumbnail · full · embedded. ⚠️ **They must not both be renderers.** Five degrees across six kinds would be thirty renderers for one thing, and each new file kind would multiply again. So the media renderer takes **two inputs**: the kind it dispatches on, the degree it is told. His front-end case — *list all the datasheets belonging to a parts list, as a link list or straight as PDFs* — is then a block collecting the media of the parts, at degree *link* or *embedded*. Free, as he wanted, and nothing new is built. | `agreed` | [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md) | extends [D-229](#) |
| D-231 | 2026-08-23 | **The preview shows what the front end will show. Its only permitted deviation is bounding the size.** The owner noticed we had never separated the two and concluded we should not: *the preview always shows how it will look in the front end. If I change something my preview may become enormous — maybe limit the size there — but it should look the way it really will.* ⚠️ **A preview that differs from the result is worse than none:** someone configures, it looks right, and publishing produces something else. Bounding the height is allowed precisely because it **claims nothing about appearance** — it crops the view rather than altering it. This sharpens [D-160](#), which said the preview renders a test data pack, without saying whose appearance it must match. | `agreed` | [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md), [20 Interaction](20-interaction.md) | sharpens [D-160](#) |

| D-232 | 2026-08-23 | **The branch decides where a value is stored, not the multiplicity. Supersedes [D-133](#).** The owner's example broke the old rule open: *if I have five integers I can simply store them there* — but under [D-133](#) a multiplicity above 1 meant **own records**, so five numbers would have become five records plus an auto-created node ([D-136](#)). Absurd for five numbers, and he felt it at once. **The new rule needs no multiplicity at all:** a target in **`Primitives`** is stored **inside** the record by path, indexed where there are several — `groessen[0]`, `groessen[1]`; a target in **`Compositions`** gets **its own records**; a target in **`Model`** is an **external reference**. **The reason underneath is the owner's own:** does the member need an identity? A row does — you point at it, order it, delete a single one. A number in a list does not. He drew the same line himself: *simple types and composed types can just be stored there; where it gets harder is whole row types, whole tables — there I would insist it is external.* **It holds on every earlier example:** *a price is not a record* — `Preis` lives under `Primitives`, so inside ✔; *a position is a thing in a list* — `Position` lives under `Compositions`, so a record ✔. ⚠️ **And it removes a cost we had accepted as honest:** [D-134](#) recorded that changing a multiplicity from 1 to `1..*` is a **storage migration**. For primitives that now disappears — only an index joins the path. The migration remains exactly where it is genuinely unavoidable, when a value becomes a row. [D-137](#)'s conversion asymmetry should be re-read against this. **Same construction as [D-161](#) and [D-183](#): the place says it.** One switch fewer, one rule fewer. | `agreed` | [50 Persistence](50-wordpress-persistence.md), [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md) | supersedes [D-133](#) |

| D-233 | 2026-08-23 | **A page renderer is fine; [D-091](#) rejected an interface, not the idea.** The owner: *I have nothing against a page renderer. I hand over a node here too and can render it, and then I have a config page renderer.* [D-091](#) says literally *`IPageRenderer` is unnecessary, **a page is a rendered node***, which refuses a **special contract** for pages, not the notion. So a configuration page renderer is an ordinary renderer whose subject happens to be a node standing for the page. No exception, no second entry point. | `agreed` | [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md) | clarifies [D-091](#) |
| D-234 | 2026-08-23 | **The block decides *what* is shown; the renderer decides *how* it is drawn.** The owner did not recognise the objection that blocks carry drawing logic — *they are consumers and they display data; whether everything is shown or not I decide in the block* — and his framing is the resolution. A block chooses the node, the attributes, the order, how many occurrences: that is **selection**, not drawing. [D-092](#) therefore stands untouched — *a node renderer renders exactly one node* — because a block is not a renderer at all but a configuration. The comparison block ([D-207](#)) fits the same way: walking up to the nearest common ancestor answers *which attributes are comparable*, which is selection again. | `agreed` | [20 Interaction](20-interaction.md), [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md) | resolves a renderer-inventory contradiction |
| D-235 | 2026-08-23 | **Grouping-renderer eligibility keys on the `data_types` binding — the proposal in [D-099](#) is now buildable.** [D-099](#) kept grouping renderers off data-type nodes: *render as a table* in the choice list of an `Integer` is noise. It needed to know what a data type **is**, and could only propose that the root be **declared** rather than hard-coded. ⚠️ **Both halves arrived since, unnoticed:** [D-193](#) split `Primitives` into **Data Types** and **Constants**, and [D-120](#) gives declared slots — the legacy settings screen already carries a `data_types` binding. So the rule reads: **grouping renderers are offered for everything that does not descend from the `data_types` binding**, and no node name appears in code, which the standard forbids. **The split pays off immediately:** `Basiseinheiten` and `Präfixe` sit under *Constants*, where a child list genuinely makes sense — and the legacy project had a `ChildListRenderer` on `Präfixe`, which under the old undivided rule would have been excluded. | `agreed` | [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md) | completes [D-099](#) |
| D-236 | 2026-08-23 | **A node carries an ordered list of renderers: one mandatory, the rest optional additions. Supersedes the single-renderer half of [D-217](#) and replaces [D-224](#)'s decorator as a configuration concept.** The owner, weighing his own earlier position: *of course I can also assign a traffic light to an int value. Perhaps instead of decorating we should simply allow several renderers — by default one, the others can be selected in addition, but there must always be one. That would probably make a good deal easier in configuration, and rendering itself would only be imperceptibly slower.* ⚠️ **This reverses his own statement of the same morning** — *a node cannot have several renderers at the same time* — and that is exactly what [D-222](#) exists for. **What is gained:** one list instead of two concepts. No *base here, decoration there*, just *this draws the value, and this as well*. In the interface it is a button rather than a notion needing explanation. He is right about the cost, too: what is expensive is queries, and none are added ([D-159](#)). **What is given up:** a decorator can **alter** the inner renderer's output — colouring the number itself when it is critical — where a list can only **append**: number, then a red dot beside it. The better trade, since *beside* covers every case named (traffic light, stars, colour rings, captions) and *alter* is rare and usually worth its own renderer anyway. **Two conditions:** the list is **ordered**, and that order is what is seen; and **exactly one is mandatory**, the owner's own rule, or a node draws nothing. ⚠️ **What survives from [D-217](#) untouched, and for a different reason:** purpose stays **out** of the registry key and in the context, because one renderer should serve display, edit and search through `supports()` rather than three being registered. A decorator remains available as an **implementation** pattern — a renderer wrapping another is still just a renderer — but it is no longer something a user configures. | `agreed` | [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md) | supersedes part of [D-217](#) and [D-224](#) |

| D-237 | 2026-08-23 | **The identifying fields belong to the type; the columns a table happens to show do not. Resolves the [D-112](#) / [D-167](#) clash.** [D-112](#) said *the shown fields are the searched fields*, and [D-167](#) writes the search column **on save** — where no use site exists, since a record does not know which lists it will later appear in. ⚠️ **Two meanings of *shown* had been conflated**, and the owner's own example separated them: *I look for a part, type `1 kΩ` and `10 %`, and get the part — or I do not find it and have to enter it. I confirm that; there is no saving involved. Saving happens later, with the row.* So [D-112](#) was about the **chooser** ([U0](20-interaction.md)): which fields let a person **recognise** this thing. That is a statement about the **type** — it does not change because some table shows two columns fewer — and it is therefore available at save time, which is what the search column needs. **The other meaning, which columns this one table displays, stays at the use site and has no effect on search at all.** ⚠️ *Worth keeping: the find-before-create step is a **confirmation**, not a save. It is part of putting a value into a reference field, and no record is written by it.* | `agreed` | [50 Persistence](50-wordpress-persistence.md), [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md) | resolves a persistence-pass contradiction |

| D-238 | 2026-08-23 | **Everything except the branch root is selectable by default. Reverses [D-181](#)'s default and keeps its insight.** ⚠️ *[D-110](#) had already refused *leaves only* — explicitly, with the owner's departments counter-case, where an intermediate node is a perfectly good answer — and allowed refusing selection only for the **branch root**. This morning's [D-181](#) made *leaves only* the default anyway. I had read the old rule and reused it without re-reading why it said what it said, which is exactly the failure the note added to [98 Documentation style](98-documentation-style.md) the same morning warns about.* **What stands from [D-181](#):** selectability belongs to the **use site**, not to the node — the real finding, and the reason the legacy `hide` control went unused, since it sat on the wrong object. **What is withdrawn:** the default. *Leaves only* would break the departments case at every new use site and have to be reopened each time. The default is **everything but the branch root**, and whoever needs leaves enforces it where they need it. **The owner's own example settles it:** entering `10 kΩ` may leave both a resistor and a resistor bridge in play — the data do not support the decision, so an intermediate node is the honest answer, not a gate to be forced past. | `agreed` | [20 Interaction](20-interaction.md), [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md) | reverses the default of [D-181](#); upholds [D-110](#) |
| D-239 | 2026-08-23 | **The chooser also works value-first: entering a value narrows the candidate types.** The owner: *the part follows from the selection — but I can also enter 10 kΩ **before** choosing anything, and then it is automatically a resistor, or something else that has 10 kΩ, a resistor bridge.* ⚠️ **The concept had only ever assumed the other direction** — pick a type, then fill in values — and nothing said the reverse was possible. It is the same chooser ([U0](20-interaction.md)) and the same search ([D-167](#)); what changes is only that the search runs **across types** rather than inside one already chosen. It also explains why [D-238](#) has to allow intermediate nodes: a value-first search narrows to *some kind of resistor* and stops there, and forcing a leaf would demand a decision the data do not contain. | `agreed` | [20 Interaction](20-interaction.md) | with [D-238](#) |

| D-240 | 2026-08-23 | **Three layers feed a preview: real data, records marked as test data, and the type's own sample value. Closes the [D-052](#) / [D-160](#) gap.** [D-052](#) had named a third source of test data — *generated from settings* — which [D-160](#) dropped in favour of *a data pack, defaults otherwise*; since packs are removable ([D-175](#)) that decided whether a preview can end up empty. The owner supplied the missing layer from the legacy project: **every data type brings its own sample** — *`int` already has the value forty-two when no other data are there, text has a sample text, a name a sample name.* **The sample belongs to the type, not to the renderer** ⚠️ *(the owner left the placement to me)*, for three reasons: since [D-236](#) a node carries **several** renderers and it would be unclear whose sample wins; a sample is a **value**, and values belong to types while a renderer draws whatever it is given; and placed on the type it serves display, edit and search alike. As a setting on the type node it follows the resolution chain, so an author may set a better sample for their own type. ⚠️ **One condition: the sample must be valid** — it has to pass its type's validators, or the preview shows something the model forbids and the fault is hunted in the wrong place. | `agreed` | [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md), [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md) | closes a renderer-pass contradiction |
| D-241 | 2026-08-23 | **The test-data mark governs front-end visibility and nothing else.** The owner: *in the data one can mark that these are test data — not yet visible in the front end, used only for testing.* In every other respect such a record is an ordinary record: it counts for uniqueness ([D-154](#)), it appears in the administration, it travels through model changes and migrations like any other. ⚠️ **Deliberately no further special-casing:** a second class of record would have to be known to every code path, which is the cost this concept has refused everywhere else. One flag, one effect. | `agreed` | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md), [20 Interaction](20-interaction.md) | with [D-240](#) |

| D-242 | 2026-08-23 | **`Ausdruck` means *printout*, not *expression* — and a printout is a frozen report.** A live collision: I had been saying *Ausdruck* for a mathematical **expression** while the owner heard the German word for **printed paper**: *do you really mean the printed paper, or the export? Is that not simply an image, a frozen moment — a screenshot of the report? That would be an `Ausdruck` for me: I create a report and print it.* In the documents nothing breaks, since the vocabulary is English ([D-187](#)) and the word there is **expression**; in German the word for it is **Formel**, and `Ausdruck` is reserved for the artefact. ⚠️ **And the reading supplied a concept the project was missing.** A **report** is live — it recomputes on every call. A **printout** is **frozen**: what stood in it then stays. Same distinction as [D-143](#), and it answers what was left open around invoices: an invoice is not a report but **a printout of one**, which is why later price corrections must not reach it. It is a document, not a window. | `agreed` | [01 Glossary](01-glossary.md), [60 Calculation](60-calculation.md) | — |

| D-243 | 2026-08-23 | **A report is a wider bracket around the tools that already exist. The expression language does not grow.** Resolves the last substantial contradiction of the calculation pass: expressions address their operands by **relative edge paths** ([D-045](#)) — *walk this edge, then that one* — which reaches everything hanging off a node, but cannot express *turnover per supplier per month*, where the things joined have **no edge between them** ([D-202](#)). ⚠️ **The answer is not a second, more powerful language.** A report is **selection + grouping + expression**: it picks a set, groups it, and the arithmetic per group is an ordinary expression, unchanged. The owner: *I would see the report as a wider bracket that uses the same tools we already have.* **Same separation as [D-234](#), one level up:** the block selects and the renderer draws; the report selects and groups, and the expression computes. **The honest price:** reports will not do everything SQL does. In exchange nobody has to learn a second language, and since reports are Release 2 ([D-203](#)) what is decided here is only **where they may not grow**. | `agreed` | [60 Calculation](60-calculation.md), [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md) | — |

| D-244 | 2026-08-23 | **The chooser defaults to the dialog; inline stays available for the simple case. Flips the default in [D-108](#).** [D-108](#) settled the shape — **two separate chooser renderers**, not one with a switch ([D-018](#)) — and made **inline** the default. Three sources then disagreed: [D-108](#) said inline, the legacy settings screen said *Default: popup*, and the owner remembered dialog. He resolved it: *in the previous project I simply left it to the user. With search, tree selection and so on we kept saying the dialog is probably the better alternative, also for searching entries. So default to the dialog, but give the user the chance to do it inline where it really is simple.* ⚠️ *Only the default moves; the construction of [D-108](#) is untouched, and the choice remains a setting on the resolution chain.* **And a clarification worth keeping:** these two renderers concern **the chooser only** — whether it sits in the form or opens as a dialog. What is drawn **after** a node is chosen is the chosen node's own renderer, which is a separate matter that had got tangled in the discussion. | `agreed` | [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md), [20 Interaction](20-interaction.md) | flips the default of [D-108](#) |
| D-245 | 2026-08-23 | **`set` and `table` were the legacy conflation of a thing with its drawing. [D-117](#) was right, and its own sentence is the resolution.** The inventory found [D-117](#) calling them *container renderers all along* while the exported tree holds them as **parked nodes with no renderer** — which looked like a contradiction and is the clean-up itself. The owner: *those are legacy baggage. We have the **compact horizontal** and **compact vertical** renderers — that corresponds roughly to `set`: a node with several attributes that I want shown as compactly as possible together. And `table` is a renderer too: it presents in table form, one row under another, every row the same columns.* So, applying [D-117](#) literally — *what a structure **looks like** is a renderer; what it **is** is a node with attributes*: a `set` **is** a composed type ([D-220](#)) and is **drawn** compactly; a `table` **is** several records of one type and is **drawn** as rows. The legacy nodes carried both roles at once, and parking them was the separation happening. **Three renderers to carry into the inventory:** compact horizontal, compact vertical, table. | `agreed` | [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md), [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md) | confirms [D-117](#) |

| D-246 | 2026-08-23 | **`set` and `table` are retired as constructs. Nothing is lost, because [D-245](#) had already moved their work elsewhere.** The owner, on being shown the split: *let us throw set and table out.* ⚠️ *Read as retiring the **constructs**, not the renderers.* What they used to do now lives in two places that already exist: the **thing** is a composed type ([D-220](#)) and the **drawing** is one of **compact horizontal**, **compact vertical** or the **table renderer**. A node that is both at once was the legacy conflation, and the exported tree had already parked them. **Consequences to carry through:** the old `Complex Datatypes` branch loses two of its three inhabitants ([D-117](#)); the legacy setting **Show set child properties** dies with them, which answers one of the six questions raised in [harvest 02](_harvest/02-settings-page.md); and the standard tree, when harvested, brings neither across. **Nothing has to be built to replace them** — that is the test a retirement has to pass, and this one passes it. | `agreed` | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md), [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md) | retires what [D-117](#) classified |

| D-247 | 2026-08-23 | **`Cleanup` stays, as maintenance for what deletion leaves behind.** The owner: *Cleanup was meant for tidying — nodes that have no connections any more, or settings that broke because something was deleted.* It is not a feature but a **repair surface**, and the concept needs one precisely because so much is derived: [D-123](#)'s two-stage deletion, [D-156](#)'s orphaned overrides, [D-159](#)'s values whose edge is gone. Each of those was decided as *leave it alone rather than tidy it silently*, which is right at the moment of the change and leaves residue over years. **Cleanup is where the residue is shown and removed deliberately**, never automatically. | `agreed` | [20 Interaction](20-interaction.md), [70 Migration](70-migration.md) | — |
| D-248 | 2026-08-23 | **One mode, not two: test mode folds into developer mode.** The legacy settings screen carried both — *Test mode* changing other defaults (confirm-delete off while testing) and *Development only* lifting deletion protection. The owner: *we do not need an extra test mode; it can simply be gathered under developer mode.* Two modes that overlap are two things to explain and two ways to be in a surprising state. The developer flag already exists as the deliberate escape from protection ([D-122](#)), and it sits at the head of the resolution chain ([D-079](#)) where a posture belongs. | `agreed` | [20 Interaction](20-interaction.md) | — |
| D-249 | 2026-08-23 | **Settings apply immediately; undo is the safety net. The save button stays available for one concrete reason.** The owner: *basically it is nicer if I make a setting and it is simply taken; at worst I can undo it.* ⚠️ **But the button is not superstition:** *it turned out that in WordPress the page sometimes jumps when I change a setting, loses focus, and I do something else.* That is a real failure of the host, not a preference, so the switch stays — immediate by default, explicit save where the environment misbehaves. Immediate saving is only defensible because undo exists ([D-172](#)); without it, *applied at once* would mean *lost at once*. | `agreed` | [20 Interaction](20-interaction.md) | — |
| D-250 | 2026-08-23 | **`Fill Model Data` and `node_presentation` are retired.** The owner on the first: *it was there to inject test data into the tree — we have our packs for that now*, so it dissolves into [D-175](#) and [D-240](#) with nothing left over. On the second: *you could assign icons or something with it, I am not even sure — we can leave it out.* ⚠️ *A construct nobody can any longer say what it was for is exactly what should not survive a restart* ([PR-1](../../CLAUDE.md)): legacy is quoted, never inherited. If a need turns up later it will arrive with a reason attached. | `agreed` | [20 Interaction](20-interaction.md), [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md) | — |
| D-251 | 2026-08-23 | **The tree shows a node's icon where one is set.** The owner: *what we should also take up is that the icon is rendered in the tree view too — that the node renderer in the tree takes the icon into account when one is present.* Small, and it is the reason the icon exists at all: the tree is where a person scans a hundred rows, and a glyph is read faster than a word. It costs nothing, since the icon is already resolved with everything else along the chain. | `agreed` | [20 Interaction](20-interaction.md), [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md) | — |

| D-252 | 2026-08-23 | **`icon` and `symbol` are two different things that merely sit next to each other.** Confirmed by the owner: *the icon is a little picture you can pick; the symbol is something like `Ω`, or `Pos.` — a very short form.* So: **icon** is a chosen glyph from the installation's allow-list, a **setting**, language-neutral, the same in every locale; **symbol** is a very short **text**, a label **role** ([D-196](#)), translated like any other label. ⚠️ *Recorded because the legacy detail screen puts them side by side in one presentation panel, which is why they had already been confused once — and because a language-neutral value in the labels table, or a translated one in the settings table, would each be wrong in a way that only shows up in the second locale.* | `agreed` | [40 I18n](40-i18n.md), [01 Glossary](01-glossary.md) | — |

| D-253 | 2026-08-23 | **Three surfaces, three jobs: the admin configures the model, the block composes a page, the front end only draws.** The owner was unsure where the line ran — *I am not so confident about the boundary between front end, Gutenberg and the settings page* — and his own description drew it. **Admin:** what a thing **is** — attributes, types, which renderers, defaults — stored in **our tables**. **Block:** what this page **shows** — which node, which record, which fields are visible — stored **in the post**, WordPress-standard, as he asked. **Front end:** nothing of its own; it draws what the block names, with the renderers resolved from the model, exactly as the admin does. ⚠️ **And that withdraws the last link of [D-226](#).** I had extended the resolution chain to *occurrence in a block* so that a value and its colour rings could stand side by side. The owner did not recognise the need — *the data already have a renderer, you would just fetch it from the registry in the front end too* — and he is right, because [D-226](#) itself already made *value, plus colour rings* **one setting at the use site**. So the chain ends at the use site, and there is no hole in the block, because nothing belongs there. **The block selects and hides; it does not choose renderers.** ⚠️ *That also makes the storage question harmless:* visibility is a statement about **this page**, so losing it when a page is copied is annoying and breaks nothing — where a renderer choice living in post content could have gone missing from the model. **Consequence:** listing one attribute twice in a block ([D-226](#)) is dropped with it; two occurrences would now render identically. | `agreed` | [20 Interaction](20-interaction.md), [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md) | withdraws the block link of [D-226](#) |
| D-254 | 2026-08-23 | **Blocks render on the server, on every request — including inside the editor.** Gutenberg lets a block either write finished HTML into the post at save time or render dynamically on each request. **Only the second is possible here**, or a page would show last year's price because it was frozen when the page was saved. And the editor must ask the **server** to render rather than drawing in JavaScript, because [D-021](#) puts renderers in PHP and [D-231](#) requires the preview to show what the front end will show. ⚠️ **The alternative — writing every renderer twice, once in PHP and once in JS — is exactly how two renderings come to disagree**, and no amount of discipline keeps them together. | `agreed` | [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md) | follows [D-021](#), [D-231](#) |

| D-255 | 2026-08-23 | **One block for a node. The layout follows the content, and whole parts can be hidden.** Asked whether his parts-list example — a form on top, a table of positions below — was a second kind of block, the owner said it is the **standard**: *if I have fixed data I show only a form; if I have fixed data and a multiplicity, I show one multiplicity after another below, as a table. Simple rules. Basically we have only one block that can display a node, and how it is displayed follows from the contents.* ⚠️ **That is a rule of the composite renderer, not of the block** ([D-234](#) stands): **a member with one value becomes a field; a member with several becomes a table**. Small fields go compactly on one line, the same compact renderer as on the settings screen ([D-245](#)). **Hiding works one level coarser than assumed:** not only single fields but **whole parts** — leave the form out and show only the positions, or show one of two multiplicities and not the other. For a composition that is also right in substance, since it exists only in connection with its whole. **Other block shapes stay possible and are not now:** the owner named a timeline as something that would arrange the same data differently. | `agreed` | [20 Interaction](20-interaction.md), [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md) | — |

| D-256 | 2026-08-23 | **The node renderer is named, and it is the same thing as the page renderer. Corrects the placement in [D-255](#).** The owner asked whether a *combi renderer, page renderer, whatever we call it* was needed for the form-plus-tables arrangement. ⚠️ *[D-255](#) had put that rule in the **composite** renderer, which was wrong: the composite renderer draws **one composed value** — `2,7 kΩ ±5 %`, number and prefix and tolerance side by side — while what the owner describes draws **a whole node with its attributes**, a form above and a positions table below.* Two different jobs at two different scales. **The rule of [D-255](#) belongs to the node renderer:** a member with one value becomes a field, a member with several becomes a table, small fields go compactly on one line. ⚠️ **And no third term is needed:** [D-233](#) and [D-091](#) already say *a page is a rendered node*, so **node renderer and page renderer are the same renderer**, used in different places. | `agreed` | [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md) | corrects [D-255](#) |
| D-257 | 2026-08-23 | **A block may override presentation settings, and such an override is page-local by declaration.** Reopens what [D-253](#) had closed, deliberately. The owner: *the block has the possibility of overriding other properties, which is more of a special case — and what is permissible we can define. Hiding matters to me in any case, but I do not see why we should forbid something that gives us more flexibility later.* **The argument is his stronger one:** the presentation is fixed in the back end and seen in the preview ([D-231](#)); the block is where one adjusts **for this one page**, and forbidding it means changing the model instead — which hits every other page. ⚠️ **The cost is stated rather than hidden:** the override lives in the post's HTML, where **nothing can query it**. *Where is this attribute shown as a colour code?* becomes unanswerable, and the conflict resolver ([D-054](#)) will not find it when the model changes. So it is allowed **and labelled page-local**, a deliberate choice rather than a trap. **Which settings may be overridden is left to be defined**, on the owner's own instruction; **visibility is certain**, the rest is a list to draw up when the block is built. | `agreed` | [20 Interaction](20-interaction.md), [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md) | reopens part of [D-253](#) |
| D-258 | 2026-08-23 | **The editor supplies the data. Entry by visitors is foreseen, has no use case, and needs no new concept.** The owner: *basically the editor provides the data for now. It may be that we also collect user data — I have no use case for it yet. That is not so bad, because we already have the rendering possibilities: we can say it is enterable by the user, and then they get it as an input.* Correct — *editable* is a circumstance of a renderer ([D-018](#)), so the mechanism exists and stays unused. ⚠️ **What is deliberately not decided with it** — who may, what is checked, how abuse is prevented — because those questions belong to the first real use case, and inventing them now would produce rules nobody can test against anything. | `agreed` | [20 Interaction](20-interaction.md) | — |

| D-259 | 2026-08-23 | **A renderer is told which label role to display. The concept had no place for this.** The owner, explaining why `St` sat in `symbol` at all: *the reason is that I need a **short form**. And then at the renderer I said I want the text as list text.* ⚠️ *That ability — telling a renderer **which** of a node's texts to show — had never been discussed in this round, and its absence is what made the `symbol` question look like a storage question.* **Every renderer brings a sensible default** — a form field `form`, a table column `table`, a compact renderer `symbol`, a tooltip `help` — and the choice is **a setting like any other**, overridable along the chain ([D-015](#)) where the default does not suit. The roles themselves are the seeded set of [D-196](#) and remain extensible ([D-151](#)). | `agreed` | [30 Renderer](30-renderer.md), [40 I18n](40-i18n.md) | — |
| D-260 | 2026-08-23 | **A unit's short form is a label in the `symbol` role. The modelled `symbol` attribute of [C44](10-domain-core.md) goes.** Resolves the contradiction raised by the interaction and domain-core catch-up: `Basiseinheit +symbol` modelled the short form as an **attribute**, while [D-252](#) makes `symbol` a translated **label role** and [40 I18n](40-i18n.md) already stores `Ω` and `St` that way — the same fact in two homes. ⚠️ **The owner proposed distinguishing by character class** — a special character is not translatable, letters are — *and it does not hold*: `kg`, `m`, `Hz`, `min` and `A` are letters and identical in every language, because they are fixed by a standard rather than abbreviated from a word. **The question is avoided rather than answered:** `symbol` is **always a label**, and the difference shows only in **how many locale rows exist**. `Ω` needs one, valid everywhere because nothing differs; `Stück` needs two, German `St` and English `pc`. One home, one resolution, no switch, and nobody has to classify a new unit when creating it. **The cost is a few more rows than strictly necessary; what it saves is the rule itself** — and with it the arguments about `°C`, `m²` and `Nm`. And with [D-259](#) the owner gets what he was actually after: give the base units their symbols, and a list renderer asks for the `symbol` role. | `agreed` | [10 Domain core](10-domain-core.md), [40 I18n](40-i18n.md) | resolves a catch-up contradiction |

| D-261 | 2026-08-23 | **A label carries a *translatable* mark; `symbol` defaults to **not**. Extends [D-260](#) rather than replacing it.** The owner: *if we simply put a flag on the symbol in the back end — translatable or not — because while I am entering it I can actually decide that.* **The value stays a label** ([D-260](#) untouched); what the mark changes is **whether translation fields are offered at all**. ⚠️ **And the gain is not saving rows, it is preventing a wrong translation.** Under [D-260](#) alone nothing stops a translator writing `kilo` into the English field of `kg`, or something *more suitable* for `Ω` — producing a unit that is wrong in one language, unnoticed by anyone not seeing both at once. **The owner's argument for the moment of entry is the strongest part:** whoever types `Ω` **knows** right then that it reads the same everywhere; later nobody knows, and a translator sees an empty field, which invites filling in. **It hangs on the individual label, not on the role** — `symbol` is language-independent on `Ohm` and not on `Stück`, the same role with two answers — and it is **changeable**, since reopening the field loses nothing. | `agreed` | [40 I18n](40-i18n.md) | extends [D-260](#) |
| D-262 | 2026-08-23 | **Of two possible defaults, take the one whose failure is noticed. `symbol` therefore defaults to *not translatable*.** The owner's reasoning, worth more than the case that produced it: *I would say for symbol, not translated to begin with. It shows in the front end if I have `Stück` and it does not say `each` in English.* ⚠️ **The asymmetry is the whole argument:** a missing translation of `Stück` is **visible** to anyone reading the English page, while `kilo` standing in for `kg` looks plausible and is seen by nobody. So the louder failure is the cheaper one, even where it is the more frequent — and *not translatable* is right for `symbol` although most symbols could have been translated harmlessly. **The default lives on the role**, not on the node: `symbol` off, `form`, `table` and `help` on. Since roles are nodes ([D-151](#)) that is an ordinary setting on the role, hard-coded nowhere, and whoever adds a role of their own sets it there. | `agreed` | [40 I18n](40-i18n.md), [20 Interaction](20-interaction.md) | with [D-261](#) |
